\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}~.

We again use the assumption that
S=\partial[p]
or
\Lambda_{i}[p]
for some
0\leq i\leq p
.
Again, the topological space
C
has a canonical structure of a compact manifold with corners such that the canonical inclusion
C_{0}\hookrightarrow C
is a properly acyclic closed union of faces.
By proper smooth approximation (e.g., as follows form
[
KM
]
) followed by genericity of transversality (Theorem 2.1 of
[
Hi
]
), homotope the pair
(f,\widetilde{f}_{0})
to one for which
C_{0}\xrightarrow{f}\Th(\xi)
is smooth and transverse to the zero-section
B\subset\Th(\xi)
.
Note that
\widetilde{f}_{0}
, then, is also smooth and transverse to the zero-section
B\subset\Th(\xi)
.
By Lemma
2.3.4
(2), there exists a smooth compactly-supported extension

\widetilde{f}\colon C\longrightarrow\Th(\xi)

of
f
that is transverse to the zero-section
B\subset\Th(\xi)
.
The restriction

\widetilde{f}_{|}\colon\mathbb{D}^{q}\times|S|\times M\hookrightarrow C\longrightarrow\Th(\xi)

is then a smooth compactly-supported map that is transverse to the zero-section
B\subset\Th(\xi)
.
Furthermore,
\widetilde{f}
witnesses a smooth homotopy through transverse maps between
f
and
\widetilde{f}_{|}
as well as a smooth homotopy through transverse maps between
\widetilde{f}_{0}
and the restriction of
\widetilde{f}_{|}
along
\mathbb{S}^{q-1}\times|S|\times M
.
Therefore, this map
\widetilde{f}_{|}
is the sought homotopy filler in the diagram (
11
).
We conclude that the map (
7
) is a weak homotopy equivalence, as desired.
∎

Proof of Lemma
2.4.2
.

Through Lemma
2.4.5
, the result is implied upon proving each of the maps between topological spaces

\displaystyle\cMap(\Delta^{p}\times M,\Th(\xi))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow

\displaystyle\cMap(\Lambda^{p}_{i}\times M,\Th(\xi))

\displaystyle\cMap^{\sf sm}(\Delta^{p}\times M,\Th(\xi))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow

\displaystyle\cMap^{\sf sm}(\Lambda^{p}_{i}\times M,\Th(\xi))

\displaystyle\cMap^{\pitchfork}(\Delta^{p}\times M,\Th(\xi))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow

\displaystyle\cMap^{\pitchfork}(\Lambda^{p}_{i}\times M,\Th(\xi))

is surjective on path-components.

The inclusion
\Lambda^{p}_{i}\hookrightarrow\Delta^{p}
is a section of a retraction between topologiacl spaces.
The resulting retraction
r:\Delta^{p}\times M\rightarrow\Lambda^{p}_{i}\times M
is a proper map.
Precomposition with
r
then defines a section of the restriction map:

\cMap(\Delta^{p}\times M,\Th(\xi))\cMap(\Lambda^{p}_{i}\times M,\Th(\xi))~.

Therefore, this restriction map is surjective on path-components, thereby proving the first case.

For the next case,
recall that the subspace
C_{0}=\Lambda^{p}_{i}\times M\subset\Delta^{p}\times M=C
is a properly acyclic closed union of faces of a manifold with corners.
Let
f\in\cMap^{\sf sm}(\Lambda^{p}_{i}\times M,\Th(\xi))
.
Lemma
2.3.4
(1) ensures the existence of a smooth compactly-supported extension:

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 19.87366pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-19.1792pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Lambda_{i}^{p}\times M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.33334pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 26.18439pt\raise 6.1111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{f}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 43.87366pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 43.87366pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Th(\xi)}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-19.87366pt\raise-31.66666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Delta^{p}\times M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 26.17316pt\raise-22.77779pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.61111pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widetilde{f}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 44.04556pt\raise-8.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces~.

This implies the sought surjectivity on path-components for the second case.

Similarly, if
f\in\cMap^{\pitchfork}(\Lambda^{p}_{i}\times M,\Th(\xi))
is a smooth compactly-supported map that is transverse with the zero-section
B\subset\Th(\xi)
, then Lemma
2.3.4
(2) ensures the existence of such a smooth extension
\widetilde{f}\in\cMap^{\pitchfork}(\Delta^{p}\times M,\Th(\xi))
that is transverse with the zero-section.
This implies the sought surjectivity on path-components for the third case.

∎

2.5.
Parametrized transversality

We now state the main result of this section.

Theorem 2.5.1
.

Each of the morphisms in (
2
) induces an equivalence between geometric realizations:

\bigl|\cMap^{\pitchfork}_{\bullet}\bigl(M,\Th(\xi)\bigr)\bigr|\xrightarrow{~\simeq~}\bigl|\cMap^{\sf sm}_{\bullet}\bigl(M,\Th(\xi)\bigr)\bigr|\xrightarrow{~\simeq~}\bigl|\cMap_{\bullet}\bigl(M,\Th(\xi)\bigr)\bigr|\xleftarrow{~\simeq~}\cMap\bigl(M,\Th(\xi)\bigr)~.

We make use of the following powerful criterion for establishing when a map between simplicial spaces induces an equivalence between geometric realizations.

Definition 2.5.2
.

A morphism
F\rightarrow G
between simplicial spaces is a
trivial Kan fibration
if, for each
p\geq 0
, the map

F([p])\longrightarrow F(\partial[p])\underset{G(\partial[p])}{\times}G([p])

is surjective on path-components.

Terminology 2.5.3
.

We will say that a morphism
F\to G
between simplicial topological spaces is a
trivial Kan fibration
if the associated morphism between simplicial spaces is.
That is, if the canonical map to the homotopy pullback is surjective on path-components, where
F(\partial[p])
and
G(\partial[p])
are the homotopy right Kan extensions of
F
and
G
to
{\sf sSet}
as in the formula (
3
).

For the proof of the following, see Proposition 6 of
[
Lu2
]
.

Lemma 2.5.4
.

Let
f\colon F\rightarrow G
be a morphism between simplicial spaces.
If
f
is a trivial Kan fibration, then the map between geometric realizations,
|F|\xrightarrow{|f|}|G|
,
is an equivalence.

∎

Lemma 2.5.5
.

The canonical morphism between simplicial spaces

\cMap^{\sm}_{\bullet}(M,\Th(\xi))\longrightarrow\cMap_{\bullet}(M,\Th(\xi))

is a trivial Kan fibration.

Proof.

By Lemma
2.4.5
, we have an equivalence

\cMap^{\sm}_{\partial[p]}\bigl(M,\Th(\xi)\bigr)\simeq\cMap^{\sm}\bigl(\partial\Delta^{p}\times M,\Th(\xi)\bigr)

and likewise for continuous mapping spaces.
Consequently, to apply Lemma
2.5.4
, it then suffices to show that for each
p\geq 0
, the map

\cMap^{\sm}(\Delta^{p}\times M,\Th(\xi))\longrightarrow\cMap^{\sm}(\partial\Delta^{p}\times M,\Th(\xi))\underset{\cMap(\partial\Delta^{p}\times M,\Th(\xi))}{\times}\cMap(\Delta^{p}\times M,\Th(\xi))

is surjective on path-components, where the righthand side is understood as the homotopy pullback.
Because each inclusion
\partial\Delta^{p}\hookrightarrow\Delta^{p}
is a cofibration, then each restriction map

\cMap(\Delta^{p}\times M,\Th(\xi))\longrightarrow\cMap(\partial\Delta^{p}\times M,\Th(\xi))

is a Serre fibration.
Therefore, the canonical map from the pullback to the homotopy pullback is a weak homotopy equivalence.
We momentarily denote the point-set pullback as

\cMap^{\sm}_{\partial\Delta^{p}\times M}(\Delta^{p}\times M,\Th(\xi))~.

Explicitly, this is a topological space consisting of compactly-supported maps
f:\Delta^{p}\times M\rightarrow\Th(\xi)
such that the restriction
f_{|M\times\partial\Delta^{p}}
is smooth.
Evidently, the map in question factors through this point-set pullback.
It thus suffices to show the canonical map

\cMap^{\sm}(\Delta^{p}\times M,\Th(\xi))\longrightarrow\cMap^{\sm}_{\partial\Delta^{p}\times M}(\Delta^{p}\times M,\Th(\xi))

is surjective on path-components.
This follows by proper smooth approximation (e.g., see
[
KM
]
): for any
f\in\cMap^{\sm}_{\partial\Delta^{p}\times M}(\Delta^{p}\times M,\Th(\xi))
, there is a homotopy
f_{t}
which is constant on
\partial\Delta^{p}\times M
and such that
f_{0}=f
and
f_{1}
is smooth.

∎

Lemma 2.5.6
.

The canonical morphism between simplicial spaces

\cMap_{\bullet}^{\pitchfork}(M,\Th(\xi))\longrightarrow\cMap^{\sf sm}_{\bullet}(M,\Th(\xi))

is a trivial Kan fibration.

Proof.

Recall by Lemma
2.4.5
, the maps

\cMap^{\sm}(\partial\Delta^{p}\times M,\Th(\xi))\longrightarrow\cMap^{\sm}_{\partial[p]}(M,\Th(\xi))

\cMap^{\pitchfork}(\partial\Delta^{p}\times M,\Th(\xi))\longrightarrow\cMap^{\pitchfork}_{\partial[p]}(M,\Th(\xi))

are weak homotopy equivalences.
So to verify the map is a trivial Kan fibration, we must verify
\pi_{0}
-surjectivity of the following map to the homotopy pullback

\textstyle{\cMap^{\pitchfork}(\Delta^{p}\times M,\Th(\xi))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cMap^{\pitchfork}(\partial\Delta^{p}\times M,\Th(\xi))\underset{\cMap^{\sm}(\partial\Delta^{p}\times M,\Th(\xi))}{\times^{\sf h}}\cMap^{\sm}(\Delta^{p}\times M,\Th(\xi))}
\textstyle{\cMap^{\sm}_{\pitchfork\partial\Delta^{p}\times M}(\Delta^{p}\times M,\Th(\xi))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

which factors through the point-set pullback, which we will temporarily denote

\cMap^{\sm}_{\pitchfork\partial\Delta^{p}\times M}(\Delta^{p}\times M,\Th(\xi))~.

Observe that the point-set pullback is exactly the subspace of
\cMap^{\sm}(\Delta^{p}\times M,\Th(\xi))
consisting of all maps
f
for which the restriction
f_{|\partial\Delta^{p}\times M}
is transverse to the zero-section
B\subset\Th(\xi)
.

To show
\pi_{0}
-surjectivity of the composite map, it therefore suffices to show that each of the factored maps are
\pi_{0}
-surjective separately.
By standard transversality (e.g., Theorem 2.1 of
[
Hi
]
), the first map

\cMap^{\pitchfork}(\Delta^{p}\times M,\Th(\xi))\hookrightarrow\cMap^{\sm}_{\pitchfork\partial\Delta^{p}\times M}(\Delta^{p}\times M,\Th(\xi))

has dense image. Since the codomain is locally path-connected, the map is surjective on path-components. The remainder of the proof establishes that the second factor,

(12)

\cMap^{\sm}_{\pitchfork\partial\Delta^{p}\times M}(\Delta^{p}\times M,\Th(\xi))\longrightarrow\cMap^{\pitchfork}(\partial\Delta^{p}\times M,\Th(\xi))\underset{\cMap^{\sm}(\partial\Delta^{p}\times M,\Th(\xi))}{\times^{\sf h}}\cMap^{\sm}(\Delta^{p}\times M,\Th(\xi))~,

is surjective on path-components.
Denote the closed interval
I:=[0,1]
.
A point in the codomain of (
12
), which is a homotopy limit, is adjoint to a compactly-supported map

I\times\partial\Delta^{p}\times M\underset{\{1\}\times\partial\Delta^{p}\times M}{\coprod}\{1\}\times\Delta^{p}\times M\xrightarrow{~f~}\Th(\xi)~,

with the following properties.

•

The restriction of
f
to each face of
\Delta^{p}\times M
is smooth.

•

For each
t\in I
, the restriction of
f
to each face of
\{t\}\times\partial\Delta^{p}\times M
is smooth.

•

The restriction of
f
to
\{0\}\times\partial\Delta^{p}\times M
is transverse to the zero-section.

By smooth approximation relative to
\partial I\times\partial\Delta^{p}\times M
, we can homotope
f
to be smooth, and so that the homotopy is constant on
\partial I\times\partial\Delta^{p}\times M
.
We may therefore assume the point in the codomain of (
12
) is adjoint to a compactly-supported map

I\times\partial\Delta^{p}\times M\underset{\{1\}\times\partial\Delta^{p}\times M}{\coprod}\{1\}\times\Delta^{p}\times M\longrightarrow\Th(\xi)

whose restriction to each face of
\Delta^{p}\times M
and of
I\times\partial\Delta^{p}\times M
is a smooth compactly-supported map.

Consider the following manifold with corners
C
, and closed union
C_{0}
of faces therein:

\Bigl(~C_{0}~\subset~C~\Bigr)~:=~\Bigl(~I\times\partial\Delta^{p}\times M\underset{\{1\}\times\partial\Delta^{p}\times M}{\coprod}\{1\}\times\Delta^{p}\times M~\subset~I\times\Delta^{p}\times M~\Bigr)~.

Observe that this pair
C_{0}\subset C
is properly acyclic.
Therefore, Lemma
2.3.4
applies to achieve a smooth extension

\widetilde{f}\colon C=I\times\Delta^{p}\times M\longrightarrow\Th(\xi)

of
f
.
Now, by assumption, the restriction of
f
along
\{0\}\times\partial\Delta^{p}\times M
is transverse to the zero-section
B\subset\Th(\xi)
.
Because
\widetilde{f}
extends
f
, the composite map

\widetilde{f}_{0}\colon\Delta^{p}\times M=\{0\}\times\Delta^{p}\times M~\hookrightarrow~I\times\Delta^{p}\times M\xrightarrow{~\widetilde{f}~}\Th(\xi)

is therefore an element in the domain of (
12
).
Consider the map

\mu\colon I\times C=I\times I\times(\Delta^{p}\times M)\xrightarrow{~m\times(\Delta^{p}\times M)~}I\times(\Delta^{p}\times M)=C~,

where
I\times I\xrightarrow{m}I
is given by
m(s,t):=st
.
The composite map,

I\times C_{0}~\hookrightarrow~I\times C\xrightarrow{~\mu~}C\xrightarrow{~\widetilde{f}~}\Th(\xi)~,

is adjoint to a path in the codomain of (
12
) to the original point from the image of
\widetilde{f}_{0}
.

∎

We can prove the main theorem of this section, our parametrized transversality theorem.

Proof of Theorem
2.5.1
.

Using Lemma
2.5.4
, Lemma
2.5.6
implies the first map is an equivalence and Lemma
2.5.5
implies the second map is an equivalence.

The map between cosimplicial topological spaces
\Delta^{\bullet}\rightarrow\ast
is by proper homotopy equivalencs.
Therefore, the canonical morphism between simplicial spaces,

\cMap(M,X)\to\cMap_{\bullet}(M,X)
,
is an equivalence.
It follows that the last map is an equivalence.

∎

3.
Codimension-
\xi
tangles in
\Delta^{\bullet}\times M

In this section, for
M
a manifold with boundary, and for
\xi
a rank-
k
vector bundle, we construct a simplicial space
\Bord^{\xi}_{\bullet}(M)
whose space of vertices is a moduli space of codimension-
\xi
tangles in
M
, and whose space of edges is a moduli space of embedded cobordisms between such.

3.1.
Codimension-
k
submanifolds

In this subsection, we fix non-negative integers
p,n,k\geq 0
, and we fix a
(p+n+k)
-manifold with corners
C
.
The key construction here is that of a moduli space
\Sub^{k}(C)
of compact codimension-
k
submanifolds of
C
.

Definition 3.1.1
.

A
codimension-
k
submanifold
of
C
is a subspace
W\subset C
such that, for each
x\in W
, there exists a smooth open embedding
\mathbb{R}^{k}\times\mathbb{R}^{j}\times\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{d}\xrightarrow{\varphi}C
such that
\varphi(0)=x
and
\varphi^{-1}(W)=\{0\}\times\mathbb{R}^{j}\times\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{d}
.

Remark 3.1.2
.

Note that, for
F\subset C
a face of
C
, then
F
is not a submanifold of
C
in the sense of Definition
3.1.1
.

Remark 3.1.3
.

Informally, a submanifold of
C
is a smooth submanifold with corners
W\subset C
such that
W\pitchfork F
for each face
F\subset C
, and each face of
W
is
W\cap F
, an intersection of
W
with a face of
C
.

Definition 3.1.4
.

Let
W
be a compact
(p+n)
-manifold with corners.

(1)

An
embedding
of
W
into
C
is a smooth embedding
f\colon W\hookrightarrow C
between manifolds with corners such that, for each
x\in W
, there exists smooth open embeddings
\mathbb{R}^{j}\times\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{d}\xrightarrow{\varphi}W
and
\mathbb{R}^{k}\times\mathbb{R}^{j}\times\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{d}\xrightarrow{\psi}C
such that
\varphi(0)=x
and such that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{R}^{j}\times\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{d}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{R}^{k}\times\mathbb{R}^{j}\times\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{d}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s,t)\mapsto(0,s,t)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

commutes.

(2)

The topological space of
embeddings
of
W
into
C
is the subspace

\Emb(W,C)~\subset~\Map^{\sm}(W,C)

of the space of smooth maps from
W
to
C
(endowed with the compact-open
\mathsf{C}^{\infty}
topology) consisting of the embeddings.

(3)

The topological space of
diffeomorphisms

\Diff(W)~\subset~\Emb(W,W)

is the subspace consisting of those embeddings that admit a smooth inverse.

Observation 3.1.5
.

Let
W\xrightarrow{f}C
be an embedding.
Its image
f(W)\subset C
is a submanifold of
C
in the sense of Definition
3.1.1
.
In particular,
f
is transverse to each face of
C
.

Definition 3.1.6
.

The topological space

\Sub^{k}(C)~:=~\left\{W\subset C\text{ codimension-$k$ submanifold}\right\}

has underlying set the compact codimension-
k
submanifolds of
M
; its topology is the finest for which, for each compact
(p+n)
-manifold with corners
W
, the map

\Emb(W,C)\xrightarrow{~{\sf Image}~}\Sub^{k}(C)~,\qquad(W\xrightarrow{f}C)\longmapsto f(W)~,

is continuous.

Observation 3.1.7
.

Let
W
be a compact
(p+n)
-manifold with corners.
Composition of diffeomorphisms defines a continuous group structure on
\Diff(W)
.
With respect to this group structure, precomposing embeddings by diffeomorphisms defines a continuous action

\Diff(W)\lacts\Emb(W,C)~.

This action is free because an embedding is, in particular, injective.

Observation 3.1.8
.

The definition of an embedding is such that, for
f,g\colon W\to C
two embeddings from a compact
(p+n)
-manifold with corners, if
{\sf Image}(f)={\sf Image}(g)
then
W\xrightarrow{g^{-1}\circ f}W
is a diffeomorphism.
Consequently, by Definition
3.1.6
, there is a canonical homeomorphism

\underset{[W]}{\coprod}\Emb(W,C)_{/\Diff(C)}\xrightarrow{~\cong~}\Sub^{k}(C)~,

from a coproduct indexed by the set of isomorphism classes of compact
(p+n)
-manifolds with corners.

Observation 3.1.9
.

The homeomorphism of Observation
3.1.8
supplies a map between spaces:

(13)

\Sub^{k}(C)\underset{\rm Obs~\ref{t15}}{\simeq}\underset{[W]}{\coprod}\Emb(W,C)_{/\Diff(W)}\longrightarrow\underset{[W]}{\coprod}\BDiff(W)~,\qquad(W\subset C)\longmapsto W~,

to the moduli space of compact
n
-manifolds with corners.

The next result gives a convenient subbasis for the topology of
\Sub^{k}(C)
.
For
W\subset C
a submanifold, its normal bundle

\nu_{W\subset C}~=~(~E(\nu_{W\subset C})\xrightarrow{~\pi~}W~)

is a smooth vector bundle.
The topological space
\Gamma^{\sf sm}(\nu_{W\subset C})
is that of smooth sections of
\nu_{W\subset C}
as it is endowed with the compact-open
\mathsf{C}^{\infty}
topology.
The tubular neighborhood theorem ensures the existence of an open embedding
E(\nu_{W\subset C})\xrightarrow{\psi}C
under
W
between manifolds with corners.
Given such, post-composition with
\psi
defines a map

(14)

\Gamma^{\sf sm}(\nu_{W\subset C})\xrightarrow{~\psi_{\ast}~}\Emb(W,C)~,\qquad\sigma\longmapsto\psi\circ\sigma~.

Lemma 3.1.10
.

Let
(W\subset C)\in\Sub^{k}(C)
be a compact codimension-
k
submanifold of
C
.
Let
E(\nu_{W\subset C})\xrightarrow{~\psi~}C
be a smooth open embedding under
W
between manifolds with corners.
The resulting map (
14
) has following properties.

(1)

The composite map

{\sf Image}\circ\psi_{\ast}\colon\Gamma^{\sf sm}(\nu_{W\subset C})\xrightarrow{~\psi_{\ast}~}\Emb(W,C)\xrightarrow{~\sf Image~}\Sub^{k}(C)~,\qquad\sigma\longmapsto\psi\Bigl(\sigma(W)\Bigr)~,

is an open embedding that carries the zero-section to
W
.
In particular,
\Sub^{k}(C)
is locally a topological vector space.

(2)

The action map

{\rm action}\colon\Diff(W)\times\Gamma^{\sf sm}(\nu_{W\subset C})\xrightarrow{~\Diff(W)\times\psi_{\ast}~}\Diff(W)\times\Emb(W,C)\xrightarrow{~\rm action~}\Emb(W,C)

fits into a pullback diagram among topological spaces:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{69.06017pt}{-1.25499pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{64}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope {}{
{}{}{}}{}{

{{{{{}}{

{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-1.03749pt}{-14.64084pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{64}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}~.

In particular, the map
\Emb(W,C)\xrightarrow{\sf Image}\Sub^{k}(C)
is a principal-
\Diff(W)
fiber bundle onto its image.

Proof.

We first prove that the map
{\sf Image}\circ\psi_{\ast}
is injective.
Let
\sigma,\tau\in\Gamma^{\sf sm}(\nu_{W\subset C})
.
Suppose
{\sf Image}\circ\psi_{\ast}(\sigma)={\sf Image}\circ\psi(\tau)
.
Because
\psi
is injective, this supposition implies
\sigma(W)=\tau(W)
.
Because both
\sigma
and
\tau
are sections in a retraction, this implies
\sigma=\tau
, as desired.

Using that the action
\Diff(W)\lacts\Emb(W,C)
is free, and that
{\sf Image}
witnesses the
\Diff(W)
-quotient, injectivity of
{\sf Image}\circ\psi_{\ast}
implies the square commutes and is a pullback among topological spaces.

It remains to prove that the map
{\sf Image}\circ\psi_{\ast}
is an open embedding.
By definition of the quotient topology, and using the pullback square, this is so if and only if the map
\Diff(W)\times\Gamma^{\sf sm}(\nu_{W\subset C})\xrightarrow{\rm action}\Emb(W,C)
is an open embedding.
Because
{\sf Image}\circ\psi_{\ast}
is injective, so is this map
{\rm action}
.
Note that the image of this map
{\rm action}
is the subspace
\Emb^{\psi}(W,C)\subset\Emb(W,C)
consisting of those embeddings
W\xrightarrow{f}C
with the following properties.

•

{\sf Image}(f)\subset{\sf Image}(\psi)
.

•

Using that
\psi
is a smooth open embedding, the resulting composite smooth map

\pi\circ\psi^{-1}\circ f\colon W\xrightarrow{f}{\sf Image}(\psi)\xrightarrow{\psi^{-1}}E(\nu_{W\subset C})\xrightarrow{\pi}W

is a diffeomorphism.

Using that the compact-open
\mathsf{C}^{\infty}
topology of
\Emb(W,C)
is finer than the compact-open topology, the subset of
\Emb(W,C)
consisting of those embeddings with the first property is open.
Now, consider the topological space
\Map^{\sf sm,\partial}(W,W)
of smooth maps from
W
to
W
that carry faces to faces, as it is endowed with the subspace of the compact-open
\mathsf{C}^{\infty}
topology.
In this topology, using that
W
is compact, the subspace
\Diff(W)\subset\Map^{\sf sm,\partial}(W,W)
is open – indeed, it is a union of connected components the submersions.
Consequently, the subset
\Emb^{\psi}(W,C)\subset\Emb(W,C)
is open.

It remains to show that the resulting continuous bijection
\Diff(W)\times\Gamma^{\sf sm}(\nu_{W\subset C})\to\Emb^{\psi}(W,C)
is a homeomorphism.
An inverse is given by the map

\Emb^{\psi}(W,C)\longrightarrow\Gamma^{\sf sm}(\nu_{W\subset C})\times\Diff(W)~,\qquad f\longmapsto\Bigl(~\pi\circ\psi^{-1}\circ f~,~\psi^{-1}\circ f\circ.\pi\circ\psi^{-1}\circ f~\Bigr)~.

∎

Lemma 3.1.11
.

The topological space
\Sub^{k}(C)
is paracompact and Hausdorff.

Proof.

The topological spaces
\Diff(W)
and
\Emb(W,C)
are each subspaces of compact-open
\mathsf{C}^{\infty}
topologies on sets of smooth maps, and are therefore metrizable, normal, paracompact, and Hausdorff.
Lemma
3.1.10
(2) implies the map
\Emb(W,C)\to\Sub^{k}(C)
is an open map.
Paracompactness and Hausdorffness of
\Sub^{k}(C)
follows.

∎

Remark 3.1.12
.

Lemma
3.1.10
(1) suggests
\Sub^{k}(C)
has a canonical structure of an infinite-dimensional smooth manifold.

3.2.
Face restriction

In this subsection, we fix non-negative integers
p,n,k\geq 0
, and we fix a
(p+n+k)
-manifold with corners
C
.
Let
D
be a manifold with corners.
Here, we show that intersection with a closed union of faces
C_{0}
of
C
determines a Serre fibration:
\Sub^{k}(C)\to\Sub^{k}(C_{0})
.

Definition 3.2.1
.

A
proper face-submersion
from
D
to
C
is a smooth map
D\xrightarrow{\sigma}C
that satisfies the following conditions.

•

The map
\sigma
is proper.

•

The image under
\sigma
of each face of
D
is a face of
C
.

•

The restriction of
\sigma
along a face of
D
is a submersion onto its image.

A
proper face-embedding
from
D
to
C
is a proper face-submersion that is an embedding.

Example 3.2.2
.

The map from the closed square to itself,

[0,1]^{2}\longrightarrow[0,1]^{2}~,\qquad(x,y)\longrightarrow(x,0)~,

is a proper face-submersion.

Example 3.2.3
.

For each morphism
[p]\xrightarrow{\sigma}[q]
in
\mathbf{\Delta}
, the map
\Delta^{p}\xrightarrow{\sigma_{\ast}}\Delta^{q}
is a proper face-submersion.
If
\sigma
is injective, then
\sigma_{\ast}
is a proper face-embedding.

Example 3.2.4
.

Let
C_{0}\subset C
be a closed union of faces which inherits the structure of a manifold with corners.
The inclusion
C_{0}\hookrightarrow C
is a proper face-embedding.
In fact, every proper face-embedding is isomorphic to such an inclusion.

Remark 3.2.5
.

Note that a proper face-embedding is not an embedding in the sense of Definition
3.1.4
.

Observation 3.2.6
.

The composition of proper face-submersions is a proper face-submersion.

Terminology 3.2.7
.

Let
D\xrightarrow{\sigma}C
be a proper face-submersion.
Let
W\subset C
be a submanifold.
We say
W
is
transverse
to
\sigma
if, for each face
E\subset D
with image face
F=\sigma(E)
, the smooth map between smooth manifolds
E\xrightarrow{\sigma_{|E}}F
is transverse to the smooth submanifold
W\cap F\subset F
.

Lemma 3.2.8
.

Each submanifold
W\subset C
is transverse to each proper face-submersion
D\xrightarrow{\sigma}C
.
Furthermore, if
W
is compact and has codimension
k
, then
\sigma^{-1}(W)\subset D
is a compact codimension-
k
submanifold of
D
.

Proof.

Let
E\subset D
be a face.
Let
F:=\sigma(E)\subset C
be its image.
The definition of a proper face-submersion is such that
F\subset C
is a face of
C
.
Furthermore, the restriction of
\sigma
along
E
factors,

\sigma_{|E}\colon E\xrightarrow{~\tau_{\sf sub}~}F\xrightarrow{~\tau_{\sf emb}~}C~,

as a submersion onto
F
followed by the inclusion of
F
.
Given Observation
3.1.5
, we have that
W
is transverse to
F
.
Consequently,
\tau_{\sf emb}\pitchfork W
and
\tau_{\sf emb}^{-1}(W)=F\cap W\subset F
is a codimension-
k
submanifold.
Next, submersions are transverse to any submanifold.
Consequently
\tau_{\sf sub}\pitchfork(\tau_{\sf emb}^{-1}(W))
and
\tau_{\sf sub}^{-1}(\tau_{\sf emb}^{-1}(W))=\sigma_{|E}^{-1}(W)\subset E
is a codimension-
k
submanifold.
This implies
\sigma
is transverse to
W
, and that
\sigma^{-1}(W)\subset D
is a codimension-
k
submanifold.
The assumed properness of
\sigma
ensures
\sigma^{-1}(W)
is compact if
W
is compact.

∎

Let
D\xrightarrow{\sigma}C
be a proper face-submersion.
Lemma
3.2.8
gives a well-defined map between sets

(15)

\Sub^{k}(C)\xrightarrow{~\sigma^{-1}(-)~}\Sub^{k}(D)~,\qquad(W\subset C)\longmapsto(\sigma^{-1}(W)\subset D)~.

Lemma 3.2.9
.

Let
D\xrightarrow{\sigma}C
be a proper face-submersion.
The map (
15
) is continuous.

Proof.

We first prove (
15
) is continuous at each element
(W\subset C)\in\Sub^{k}(C)
.
Through the transversality statement of Lemma
3.2.8
, and the fact that normal bundles pull back along transverse maps, there is a canonical isomorphism between vector bundles over
\sigma^{-1}(W)
:

\nu_{\sigma^{-1}(W)\subset D}\overset{\alpha}{~\cong~}(\sigma_{|\sigma^{-1}(W)})^{\ast}\nu_{W\subset C}~.

Such an isomorphism determines a canonical map

(16)

\Gamma^{\sf sm}(\nu_{W\subset C})\longrightarrow\Gamma^{\sf sm}\Bigl({\sigma_{|\sigma^{-1}(W)}}^{\ast}\nu_{W\subset C}\Bigr)~\cong~\Gamma^{\sf sm}(\nu_{\sigma^{-1}(W)\subset D})~.

Next, choose compatible tubular neighborhoods for
W\subset C
and for
\sigma^{-1}(W)\subset D
.
Specifically, choose smooth open embeddings
E(\nu_{\sigma^{-1}(W)\subset D})\xrightarrow{\psi^{D}}D
and
E(\nu_{W\subset C})\xrightarrow{\psi^{C}}C
between manifolds with corners, respectively under
\sigma^{-1}(W)
and under
W
, such that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E(\nu_{\sigma^{-1}(W)\subset D})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E(\nu_{W\subset C})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi^{D}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pr\circ\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi^{C}}

commutes and is a pullback.
By way of this commutative pullback diagram, observe that the diagram among sets

(17)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Gamma^{\sf sm}(\nu_{W\subset C})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Gamma^{\sf sm}(\nu_{\sigma^{-1}(W)\subset D})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Sub^{k}(C)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Sub^{k}(D)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\ref{e58})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\sf Image}\circ\psi^{C}_{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\sf Image}\circ\psi^{D}_{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma^{-1}(-)}

commutes.
Lemma
3.1.10
(1) states that the vertical maps in this diagram are open embeddings, which carry the respective zero-sections to
(W\subset C)
and to
(\sigma^{-1}(W)\subset D)
.
Continuity of (
16
) therefore implies continuity of
\sigma^{-1}(-)
about
W
, as desired.

∎

Lemma 3.2.10
.

Let
D\xrightarrow{\sigma}C
be a proper face-embedding.
The map
\Sub^{k}(C)\xrightarrow{\sigma^{-1}(-)}\Sub^{k}(D)
is a Serre fibration.

Proof.

Let
W
be a compact manifold with corners.
For

Lemma
3.2.8
implies
\sigma^{\ast}W
is a manifold with corners, and
\sigma^{\ast}f
is a codimension-
k
embedding; furthermore,
\sigma_{|W}
is a proper face-embedding.
Therefore,
\sigma_{|W}
canonically factors

\sigma_{|W}\colon\sigma^{\ast}W\xrightarrow{~\cong~}W_{\sigma}\xrightarrow{~\rm inclusion~}W

as a diffeomorphism onto a closed union of faces
W_{\sigma}\subset W
that is, itself, a compact manifold with corners.

Let
W_{0}\subset W
be a closed union of faces that is, itself, a compact manifold with corners.
Consider the subset
\Emb^{[W_{0}]}(W,C)\subset\Emb(W,C)
consisting of those embeddings
W\xrightarrow{f}C
such that the two closed unions of faces
W_{\sigma}=W_{0}
agree.
Observe the identity between subsets of
D
:

(18)

\sigma^{-1}(f(W))~=~(\sigma^{\ast}f)(W_{\sigma})~.

As there are only finite many closed unions of faces of
W
, this subset
\Emb^{[W_{0}]}(W,C)\subset\Emb(W,C)
is evidently a union of connected components.
Observe the filler in the diagram among sets,

(19)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Emb^{[W_{0}]}(W,C)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Emb(W,C)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Emb(W_{0},D)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Map^{\sf emb}(W_{0},C)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm inclusion}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm restriction}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma\circ-}

forming a pullback.
Here,
\Map^{\sf emb}(W_{0},C)\subset\Map^{\sf sm}(W,C)
is the subspace consisting of those smooth maps that are locally of the form
\{0\}\times\mathbb{R}^{j}\times\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{S}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{k}\times\mathbb{R}^{j}\times\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{d}
for some
j,d\geq 0
and some finite subset
S\subset\{1,\dots,d\}
.
2
2

2

Recall that the proper face-embedding
D\hookrightarrow C
is not an embedding in the sense of Definition
3.1.4
. Consequently, if
W_{0}\hookrightarrow D
is an embedding, then the composite
W_{0}\hookrightarrow D\hookrightarrow C
will only satisfy the local condition given, defining an element of
\Map^{\sf emb}(W_{0},C)
but
not
of
\Emb(W_{0},C)
.

Because
\sigma
is an embedding, the lower map is a monomorphism between topological spaces.
The two other solid maps are manifestly continuous.
Therefore, the dashed function is continuous, and the square is a pullback among topological spaces.
It follows that the function

(20)

\Emb(W,C)\longrightarrow\underset{W_{0}\subset W}{\coprod}\Emb(W_{0},D)~,\qquad(W\xrightarrow{f}C)\longmapsto\Bigl(W_{\sigma}\cong\sigma^{\ast}\xrightarrow{\sigma^{\ast}f}D\Bigr)~,

is continuous,
where the coproduct is indexed by the finite set of closed unions of faces that are, themselves, compact manifolds with corners.
Taking coproducts gives a map

(21)

\underset{[W]}{\coprod}\Emb(W,C)\xrightarrow{~\underset{[W]}{\coprod}(\ref{e61})~}\underset{[W_{0}\subset W]}{\coprod}\Emb(W_{0},D)\xrightarrow{~{\rm forget}~W{\rm s}~}\underset{[W_{0}]}{\coprod}\Emb(W_{0},D)~.

Now, the isotopy extension theorem (e.g.,
[
Pa
]
) implies the right vertical map in (
19
) is a fiber bundle.
Because the square is a pullback, the left vertical map in (
19
) is a fiber bundle as well.
In fact, as in Palais’s proof of the isotopy extension theorem in
[
Pa
]
, each of these embedding spaces is locally contractible.
3
3

3

Given
(W_{0}\xrightarrow{f}D)\in\Emb(W_{0},D)
, a basis for the topology of
\Emb(W_{0},D)
about
f_{0}
consisting of contractible open subsets can be as follows.
Choose a complete Riemannian metric on
C
that is a product metric in a neighborhood of each face of
C
.
This Riemannian metric pulls back along
\sigma
as one on
D
, with respect to which
\sigma
is a totally geodesic isometric embedding.
The exponential map defines a map
\Gamma^{\sf sm}(f_{0}^{\ast}\tau_{D})\xrightarrow{\sf exp}\Emb(W_{0},D)
that carries the zero-section to
f_{0}
.
This map restricts as an open embedding along some convex open neighborhood about the zero-section.
Through this open embedding, the images of all convex open subsets about the zero-section supplies the desired basis about
f_{0}
.

Therefore, there exists an open cover of
\Emb(W_{0},D)
by contractible subspaces.
Such an open cover is necessarily a trivializing open cover for any fiber bundle over
\Emb(W_{0},D)
.
We conclude that the map (
21
) is a fiber bundle.

The identity (
18
) implies the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underset{[W]}{\coprod}\Emb(W,C)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underset{[W_{0}]}{\coprod}\Emb(W_{0},D)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Sub^{k}(C)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Sub^{k}(D)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\ref{e64})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\sf Image}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\sf Image}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma^{-1}(-)}

commutes.
Lemma
3.1.10
(2) states that the vertical maps in this diagram are fiber bundles.
Because the composition of fiber bundles is a fiber bundle, we conclude that the map
\underset{[W]}{\coprod}\Emb(W,C)\to\Sub^{k}(D)
is a fiber bundle.
Lemma
3.2.11
, stated and proved below, then completes this proof.

∎

Lemma 3.2.11
.

Let
A\xrightarrow{f}B\xrightarrow{g}C
be a pair of composable maps between topological spaces.
Suppose
f
is surjective.
If
f
and
g\circ f
are Serre fibrations, then
g
is a Serre fibration.

Proof.

Recall that a map is a Serre fibrations if and only if it has the right lifting property with respect to the inclusion
\mathbb{D}^{q}\xrightarrow{0}\mathbb{D}^{q}\times I
for all
q\geq 0
, where
I=[0,1]
is the closed unit interval and the map
0
is the inclusion of the subspace
\mathbb{D}^{q}\times\{0\}
.
So let
q\geq 0
.
Let
\mathbb{D}^{q}\xrightarrow{v}B
and
\mathbb{D}^{q}\times I\xrightarrow{\widetilde{w}}C
be maps fitting into a solid commutative diagram among topological spaces:

Here, the map
\mathbb{S}^{q-1}\times I\xrightarrow{s}\mathbb{D}^{q}
is the sphereical coordinates map:
s(p,t):=tp
.
The upper left square is therefore a pushout.
Now, using that
f
is assumed surjective, choose a map
\ast\xrightarrow{a}A
making the upper right square commute – such a choice is simply a preimage of
v(0)\in B
.
Using that Serre fibrations have the right lifting property with respect to the map
\mathbb{S}^{q-1}\xrightarrow{0}\mathbb{S}^{q-1}\times I
, choose a map
\mathbb{S}^{q-1}\times I\xrightarrow{\widetilde{a}}A
making the upper left triangle commute.
Using that the upper left square is a pushout, the upper left triangle determines a map
u
filling the upper right square.
Using that
g\circ f
is assumed a Serre fibration, choose a map
\widetilde{u}
as in the commutative diagram.
Finally, take
\widetilde{v}:=f\circ\widetilde{u}
.
∎

3.3.
Codimension-
k
tangles in
\Delta^{\bullet}\times M

In this subsection, we fix
n,k\geq 0
and an
(n+k)
-manifold
M
with boundary.
The key construction here is that of a simplicial topological space
\Bord^{k}_{\bullet}(M)
whose topological space of
[p]
-points is the moduli space
\Sub^{k}(\Delta^{p}\times M)
of compact codimension-
k
submanifolds of
\Delta^{p}\times M
.

Observation 3.3.1
.

Let
M
be a manifold with boundary.
For morphism
[p]\xrightarrow{\sigma}[q]
in
\mathbf{\Delta}
, the map

\Delta^{p}\times M\xrightarrow{~\sigma_{\ast}\times M~}\Delta^{q}\times M

is a proper face-submersion.

Notation 3.3.2
.

For each
p\geq 0
, denote the topological space

\Bord^{k}_{p}(M)~:=~\Sub^{k}(\Delta^{p}\times M)~.

Lemma 3.3.3
.

The assignment

[p]\longmapsto\Bord^{k}_{p}(M)

assembles as a simplicial topological space:

\Bord^{k}_{\bullet}(M)\colon\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}\longrightarrow\Top~.

Proof.

What remains is to define this functor on morphisms, then check that the assignment respects composition. Let
[p]\xrightarrow{\sigma}[q]
be a morphism in
\mathbf{\Delta}
.
Using Observation
3.3.1
, Lemma
3.2.9
implies the map

\sigma^{\ast}\colon\Bord^{k}_{q}(M)=\Sub^{k}(\Delta^{q}\times M)\xrightarrow{~(\sigma_{\ast}\times M)^{-1}(-)~}\Sub^{k}(\Delta^{p}\times M)=\Bord^{k}_{p}(M)~,

(22)

(W\subset\Delta^{q}\times M)\longmapsto\Bigl((\sigma_{\ast}\times M)^{-1}(W)\subset\Delta^{p}\times M\Bigr)~,

is well-defined and continuous.

For
[\ell]\xrightarrow{\tau}[p]
another morphism in
\mathbf{\Delta}
,
observe the identity between subspaces of
\Delta^{\ell}\times M
:

((\sigma\tau)_{\ast}\times M)^{-1}(W)\subset\Delta^{p}\times M~=~(\tau_{\ast}^{-1}\times M\Bigl((\sigma_{\ast}\times M)^{-1}(W)\Bigr)~.

This identity immediately implies the named assignments respect composition:
(\sigma\circ\tau)^{\ast}=\tau^{\ast}\circ\sigma^{\ast}
.

∎

3.4.
Codimension-
\xi
submanifolds

In this subsection, we fix non-negative integers
p,n,k\geq 0
, and we fix a
(p+n+k)
-manifold with corners
C
.
Here, we construct a universal fiber bundle
\widetilde{\Sub}^{k}(C)
over the moduli space
\Sub^{k}(C)
, as it is equipped with a normal vector bundle.
We use this to define the moduli space
\Sub^{\xi}(C)
of compact codimension-
\xi
submanifolds of
C
.

Consider the subspace

\widetilde{\Sub}^{k}(C)~:=~\left\{\Bigl(W\subset C,x\in C\Bigr)\mid x\in W\right\}~\subset~\Sub^{k}(C)\times C~.

Projection onto the first coordinate defines a map

{\sf fgt}\colon\widetilde{\Sub}^{k}(C)\longrightarrow\Sub^{k}(C)~,\qquad(W\subset C,x\in W)\longmapsto(W\subset C)~,

whose fiber over
(W\subset C)
is
W
.
Evaluation defines a map

(23)

\ev\colon\Emb(W,C)\times W\longrightarrow\widetilde{\Sub}^{k}(C)~,\qquad(f,x)\longmapsto\Bigl(f(W),f(x)\Bigr)~,

which fits into a commutative diagram among topological spaces:

Observation 3.4.1
.

(1)

The topology of
\widetilde{\Sub}^{k}(C)
is the finest for which, for each compact
(p+n)
-manifold
W
with corners, the map (
23
) is continuous.
Consequently, the canonical map,

\underset{[W]}{\coprod}\Emb(W,C)\underset{\Diff(W)}{\times}W\xrightarrow{~\cong~}\widetilde{\Sub}^{k}(C)~,

is a homeomorphism.

(2)

In particular, after Lemma
3.1.10
(2), the map

\widetilde{\Sub}^{k}(C)\to\Sub^{k}(C)

has the structure of a fiber bundle of compact
(p+n)
-manifolds with corners.

(3)

Furthermore, the topological subspace

(24)

\widetilde{\Sub}^{k}(C)~\hookrightarrow~\Sub^{k}(C)\times C

has the structure of a fiber bundle over
\Sub^{k}(C)
of compact comimension-
k
submanifolds of
C
.

The fiberwise (over
\Sub^{k}(C)
) normal bundle of (
24
) is a rank-
k
vector bundle over
\widetilde{\Sub}^{k}(C)
:

(25)

\nu^{\sf fib}(C)~:=~\Bigl(E(\nu^{\sf fib}(C))\to\widetilde{\Sub}^{k}(C)\Bigr)~;

By construction, its fiber over
(x\in W\subset C)
is the normal vector space
\mathsf{N}_{x}
of
W\subset C
at
x
.
This rank-
k
vector bundle is classified by a map between spaces:

(26)

\nu^{\sf fib}(C)\colon\widetilde{\Sub}^{k}(C)\longrightarrow\BO(k)~.

.

3.4.1

13
).

26
) supplies a lift among spaces:

Remark 3.4.4
.

Explicitly, a point in
\Sub^{k}(C)
is a codimension-
k
submanifold
W\subset C
, together with a map
g\colon W\rightarrow B
, as well as an isomorphism
\nu_{W\subset C}\cong g^{\ast}\xi
between vector bundles over
W
.

Observation 3.4.5
.

In the case that
\xi=\gamma_{k}
is the universal rank-
k
vector bundle over
\BO(k)
, the canonical map between spaces

\Sub^{\gamma_{k}}(C)\xrightarrow{~\simeq~}\Sub^{k}(C)

is an equivalence since the right vertical map in Definition
3.4.3
is an equivalence for
B=\BO(k)
.

Forgetting smooth structure defines a functor between
\infty
-categories,
\BDiff(W)\to\Spaces
, for each compact
n
-manifold
W
with corners.
Post-composing the map (
13
) with such functors defines a functor between
\infty
-categories,

\Sub^{k}(C)\longrightarrow\Spaces~,

which classifies the fibration underlying the fiber bundle of Observation
3.4.1
(2).
The map (
26
), in turn, supplies a lift of this functor:

(27)

\Sub^{k}(C)\xrightarrow{~\nu^{\sf fib}(C)~}\Spaces_{/\BO(k)}~.

This lift affords an alternative description of the space
\Sub^{\xi}(C)
as the pullback among
\infty
-categories:

3.5.
Codimension-
\xi
tangles in
\Delta^{\bullet}\times M

In this subsection, we fix
n,k\geq 0
and an
(n+k)
-manifold
M
with boundary; we fix a rank-
k
vector bundle
\xi=(E\to B)
, and we denote the map classifying it again as
B\xrightarrow{\xi}\BO(k)
.
The key construction here is that of a simplicial space
\Bord^{\xi}_{\bullet}(M)
whose space of
[p]
-points is the moduli space
\Sub^{\xi}(\Delta^{p}\times M)
of compact codimension-
\xi
submanifolds of
M
.

The proof of the following technical result is §
3.6
.
Its statement references the
\infty
-category

\mathbf{\Delta}_{/\Bord^{k}_{\bullet}(M)}~,

which is the
\infty
-overcategory of
\Bord^{k}_{\bullet}(M)
with respect to the Yoneda functor
\mathbf{\Delta}\to\PShv(\mathbf{\Delta})
.
The canonical projection

\mathbf{\Delta}_{/\Bord^{k}_{\bullet}(M)}\xrightarrow{~\rm forget~}\mathbf{\Delta}

is therefore a right fibration; it is the unstraightening of the functor
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}\xrightarrow{\Bord^{k}_{\bullet}(M)}\Spaces
.
In particular, its fiber over
[p]
is the space
\Bord^{k}_{p}(M)
of
p
-simplices of the simplicial space
\Bord^{k}_{\bullet}(M)
.

Lemma 3.5.1
.

There is a functor

\nu^{\sf fib}_{\bullet}(M)\colon\mathbf{\Delta}_{/\Bord^{k}_{\bullet}(M)}\longrightarrow\Spaces_{/\BO(k)}

whose restriction to the fiber of
\mathbf{\Delta}_{/\Bord^{k}_{\bullet}(M)}\xrightarrow{\rm forget}\mathbf{\Delta}
over
[p]
is the map

\nu^{\sf fib}_{p}(M)\colon\Bord^{k}_{p}(M)=\Sub^{k}(\Delta^{p}\times M)\xrightarrow{\nu^{\sf fib}(\Delta^{p}\times M)}\Spaces_{/\BO(k)}

from (
27
).

Notation 3.5.2
.

We denote the pullback
\infty
-category:

Observation 3.5.3
.

The composite functor
\Un^{\xi}(M)\to\mathbf{\Delta}_{/\Bord^{k}_{\bullet}(M)}\to\mathbf{\Delta}
is right fibration. Indeed, the base-change of a right fibrations is a right fibration, and the composition of right fibrations is a right fibration.

Definition 3.5.4
.

The simplicial space of
codimension-
\xi
tangles in
\Delta^{\bullet}\times M
,

\Bord^{\xi}_{\bullet}(M)\colon\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}\longrightarrow\Spaces~,

is that classifying the right fibration
\Un^{\xi}(M)\to\mathbf{\Delta}
.

Remark 3.5.5
.

Equivalently,
\Bord^{\xi}_{\bullet}(M)
is the simplicial space equipped with an identification
\Un^{\xi}(M)\simeq\mathbf{\Delta}_{/\Bord^{\xi}_{\bullet}(M)}
over
\mathbf{\Delta}
. That is, the unstraightening of
\Bord^{\xi}_{\bullet}(M)
is
\Un^{\xi}(M)\to\mathbf{\Delta}
.

Observation 3.5.6
.

By Definition
3.4.3
as a pullback, Lemma
3.5.1
reveals, for each
p\geq 0
, a canonical identification between spaces:

\Bord^{\xi}_{p}(M)~\simeq~\Sub^{\xi}(\Delta^{p}\times M)~.

Remark 3.5.7
.

A 0-simplex in
\Bord_{\bullet}^{\xi}(M)
is a compact
n
-submanifold
W\subset M
, together with a map
g\colon W\rightarrow B
, and an isomorphism between vector bundles
\nu_{W\subset M}\cong g^{\ast}\xi
from the normal bundle to the pullback bundle.
A 1-simplex in
\Bord^{\xi}_{\bullet}(M)
is an embedded cobordism between such. Generally, a
p
-simplex in
\Bord_{\bullet}^{\xi}(M)
is a compact
(n+p)
-submanifold
W\subset\Delta^{p}\times M
, together with a map
g\colon W\rightarrow B
, and an isomorphism between vector bundles
\nu_{W\subset\Delta^{p}\times M}\cong g^{\ast}\xi
from the normal bundle to the pullback bundle.

Observation 3.5.8
.

In the case that
\xi=\gamma_{k}
is the universal rank-
k
vector bundle over
\BO(k)
, Observation
3.4.5
implies the canonical morphism between simplicial spaces

\Bord^{\gamma_{k}}_{\bullet}(M)\xrightarrow{~\simeq~}\Bord^{k}_{\bullet}(M)

is an equivalence.

3.6.
Proof of Lemma
3.5.1

A priori, constructing a functor to the
\infty
-category
\Spaces_{/\BO(k)}
requires an infinite amount of homotopy coherence.
For the case at hand, we exploit the geometry intrinsic to
\Bord^{k}_{\bullet}(M)
to resolve this a priori infinite homotopy coherence problem.
Specifically, we implement the following.

•

We construct a topological category
\Un^{k}(M)
whose underlying
\infty
-category is
\mathbf{\Delta}_{/\Bord^{k}_{\bullet}(M)}
.

•

We construct a topological category
\widetilde{\Un}^{k}(M)
equipped with a functor to
\Un^{k}(M)
whose underlying functor between
\infty
-categories is a left fibration.

•

We construct a vector bundle
\nu^{\sf fib}_{\bullet}(M)
over
\widetilde{\Un}^{k}(M)
, in the sense of §
6
.

The vector bundle
\nu^{\sf fib}_{\bullet}(M)
is classified by a functor from the underlying
\infty
-category,
\widetilde{\Un}^{k}(M)\to\BO(k)
, which thereafter straightens as a functor from the underlying
\infty
-category,
\mathbf{\Delta}_{/\Bord^{k}_{\bullet}(M)}\simeq\Un^{k}(M)\to\Spaces_{/\BO(k)}
.
Tracking this construction reveals that its restriction to
\Bord^{k}_{p}(M)
is as desired.

Consider the Grothendieck construction (also known as the unstraightening) of the functor
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}\xrightarrow{\Bord^{k}_{\bullet}(M)}\Top
of Lemma
3.3.3
:

\Un^{k}(M):=\Un\Bigl(\Bord^{k}_{\bullet}(M)\Bigr)\longrightarrow\mathbf{\Delta}~.

Explicitly, it is a category-object in
\Top
over
\mathbf{\Delta}
given as follows.

•

Its topological space of objects over an object
[p]\in\mathbf{\Delta}
is

\Obj\Bigl(\Un^{k}(M)\Bigr)_{|[p]}~:=~\Bord^{k}_{p}(M)~.

•

Its topological space of morphisms over a morphism
[p]\xrightarrow{\sigma}[q]
in
\mathbf{\Delta}
is

\Mor\Bigl(\Un^{k}(M)\Bigr)_{|\sigma}~:=~\Bord^{k}_{q}(M)~.

•

Its source and target maps,

\Obj\Bigl(\Un^{k}(M)\Bigr)\xleftarrow{~\rm source~}\Mor\Bigl(\Un^{k}(M)\Bigr)\xrightarrow{~\rm target~}\Obj\Bigl(\Un^{k}(M)\Bigr)~,

are given over a morphism
[p]\xrightarrow{\sigma}[q]
in
\mathbf{\Delta}
as

\Bord^{k}_{p}(M)\xleftarrow{~\sigma^{\ast}~}\Bord^{k}_{q}(M)\xrightarrow{~=~}\Bord^{k}_{q}(M)~.

•

The composition rule is the map,

\Mor\Bigl(\Un^{k}(M)\Bigr)\underset{\Obj\Bigl(\Un^{k}(M)\Bigr)}{\times}\Mor\Bigl(\Un^{k}(M)\Bigr)\xrightarrow{~\circ~}\Mor\Bigl(\Un^{k}(M)\Bigr)

given over a composable pair of morphisms
[p]\xrightarrow{\sigma}[q]\xrightarrow{\tau}[r]
in
\mathbf{\Delta}
by

\Bord^{k}_{r}(M)\xrightarrow{~\tau^{\ast}~}\Bord^{k}_{q}(M)~.

Observation 3.6.1
.

(1)

The target morphism for
\Un^{k}(M)
is evidently a covering space.
Therefore
\Un^{k}(M)
is a topological category in the sense of §
6
.

(2)

The functor
\Un^{k}(M)\to\mathbf{\Delta}
is a right fibration between topological categories, which is to say commutative square of topological spaces

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Mor(\Un^{k}(M))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Obj(\Un^{k}(M))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Mor(\mathbf{\Delta})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Obj(\mathbf{\Delta})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm target}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm target}}

is a pullback.

(3)

The functor between
\infty
-categories underlying
\Un^{k}(M)\to\mathbf{\Delta}
is the right fibration
\mathbf{\Delta}_{/\Bord^{k}_{\bullet}(M)}\to\mathbf{\Delta}
classified by the simplicial space
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}\xrightarrow{\Bord^{k}_{\bullet}(M)}\Top\xrightarrow{\rm forget}\Spaces
.

The universal fiber bundles from §
3.4
are functorial in a certain sense.
For starters, for each morphism
[p]\xrightarrow{\sigma}[q]
in
\mathbf{\Delta}
, we have a commutative diagram among topological spaces,

(28)

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}~,

in which the left square is a pullback and the upper rightward map is given by

\sigma_{\ast}\times M\colon\Bigl(W\subset\Delta^{q}\times M,x\in(\sigma_{\ast}\times M)^{-1}(W)\Bigr)\longmapsto\Bigl(W\subset\Delta^{q}\times M,(\sigma_{\ast}\times M)(x)\in W\Bigr)~.

Better, this functoriality may be organized as a category-object in
\Top
over

\Un^{k}(M)
,

\widetilde{\Un}^{k}(M)\longrightarrow\Un^{k}(M)~,

which we now define.

•

Its topological space of objects over an object
[p]\in\mathbf{\Delta}
is

\Obj\Bigl(\widetilde{\Un}^{k}(M)\Bigr)_{|[p]}~:=~\widetilde{\Bord}^{k}_{p}(M)~.

•

Its topological space of morphisms over a morphism
[p]\xrightarrow{\sigma}[q]
in
\mathbf{\Delta}
is

\Mor\Bigl(\widetilde{\Un}^{k}(M)\Bigr)_{|\sigma}~:=~\widetilde{\Bord}^{k}_{p}(M)\underset{\Bord^{k}_{p}(M)}{\times}\Bord^{k}_{q}(M)~.

•

Its source and target maps,

\Obj\Bigl(\widetilde{\Un}^{k}(M)\Bigr)\xleftarrow{~\rm source~}\Mor\Bigl(\widetilde{\Un}^{k}(M)\Bigr)\xrightarrow{~\rm target~}\Obj\Bigl(\widetilde{\Un}^{k}(M)\Bigr)~,

are given over a morphism
[p]\xrightarrow{\sigma}[q]
in
\mathbf{\Delta}
as

\widetilde{\Bord}^{k}_{p}(M)\xleftarrow{~\pr~}\widetilde{\Bord}^{k}_{p}(M)\underset{\Bord^{k}_{p}(M)}{\times}\Bord^{k}_{q}(M)\xrightarrow{~\sigma_{\ast}\times M~}\widetilde{\Bord}^{k}_{q}(M)~.

•

The composition rule is the map,

\Mor\Bigl(\widetilde{\Un}^{k}(M)\Bigr)\underset{\Obj\Bigl(\widetilde{\Un}^{k}(M)\Bigr)}{\times}\Mor\Bigl(\widetilde{\Un}^{k}(M)\Bigr)\xrightarrow{~\circ~}\Mor\Bigl(\widetilde{\Un}^{k}(M)\Bigr)

given over a composable pair of morphisms
[p]\xrightarrow{\sigma}[q]\xrightarrow{\tau}[r]
in
\mathbf{\Delta}
by

\widetilde{\Bord}^{k}_{p}(M)\underset{\Bord^{k}_{p}(M)}{\times}\Bord^{k}_{r}(M)\xrightarrow{~\id\times\tau^{\ast}~}\widetilde{\Bord}^{k}_{p}(M)\underset{\Bord^{k}_{p}(M)}{\times}\Bord^{k}_{q}(M)~.

Observation 3.6.2
.

The projection
\widetilde{\Bord}^{k}_{p}(M)\to\Bord^{k}_{p}(M)
for each
p\geq 0
defines a morphism
\widetilde{\Un}^{k}(M)\to\Un^{k}(M)
between category-objects in
\Top
.
Furthermore, the definition of
\widetilde{\Un}^{k}(M)
is such that
\widetilde{\Un}^{k}(M)\to\Un^{k}(M)
is a left fibration between category-objects internal to
\Top
, which is to say the commutative square of topological spaces

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Mor(\widetilde{\Un}^{k}(M))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Obj(\widetilde{\Un}^{k}(M))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Mor(\Un^{k}(M))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Obj(\Un^{k}(M))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm source}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm source}}

is a pullback.

Lemma 3.6.3
.

\widetilde{\Un}^{k}(M)
is a topological category in the sense of §
6
.
Moreover, the functor between
\infty
-categories underlying
\widetilde{\Un}^{k}(M)\to\Un^{k}(M)
is a left fibration.
Furthermore, this left fibration is classified by a functor

(29)

\Un^{k}(M)\simeq\mathbf{\Delta}_{/\Bord^{k}_{\bullet}(M)}\longrightarrow\Spaces

whose restriction to
\Bord^{k}_{p}(M)\subset\Un^{k}(M)
classifies the fibration
\widetilde{\Bord}^{k}_{p}(M)\to\Bord^{k}_{p}(M)
and whose coCartesian monodromy functors are given by the maps
\sigma_{\ast}\times M
from (
28
).

Proof.

Provided
\widetilde{\Un}^{k}(M)
is a topological category, Observation
3.6.2
then implies
\widetilde{\Un}^{k}(M)\to\Un^{k}(M)
is a left fibration between
\infty
-categories.
The description of spaces over
\Bord^{k}_{p}(M)
then follows directly from the definition of
\widetilde{\Un}^{k}(M)
.
So it remains to verify
\widetilde{\Un}^{k}(M)
is a topological category.

We must prove, for each
r\geq 0
, that the canonical map

(30)

\Hom([r],\widetilde{\Un}^{k}(M))\longrightarrow\Hom^{\sf h}([r],\widetilde{\Un}^{k}(M))

is a weak homotopy equivalence.
The continuous functor
\widetilde{\Un}^{k}(M)\to\Un^{k}(M)
determines a commutative diagram among topological spaces:

(31)

\textstyle{\Hom([r],\widetilde{\Un}^{k}(M))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Hom^{\sf h}([r],\widetilde{\Un}^{k}(M))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Hom([r],\Un^{k}(M))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Hom^{\sf h}([r],\Un^{k}(M)).}

Unwinding the definition of the continuous functor
\widetilde{\Un}^{k}(M)\to\Un^{k}(M)
, Observation
3.4.1
(3) reveals that left vertical map in this square is a fiber bundle, and its fiber over such a point is the topological space
W
.
Now, because
\widetilde{\Un}^{k}(M)\to\Un^{k}(M)
is a left fibration between category-objects in
\Top
, for each
0\leq i<r
, the commutative square among topological spaces

\textstyle{\Hom(\{i<i+1\},\widetilde{\Un}^{k}(M))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Hom(\{i\},\widetilde{\Un}^{k}(M))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Hom(\{i<i+1\},\Un^{k}(M))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Hom(\{i\},\Un^{k}(M))}

is a pullback.
Furthermore, Observation
3.4.1
(3) implies the vertical maps in this diagram are fiber bundles.
Consequently, the canonical map from each fiber of the left vertical map in (
31
) to the corresponding fiber of the right vertical map in (
31
) is a weak homotopy equivalence.
The 5-Lemma then implies the map (
30
) is a weak homotopy equivalence, as desired.

∎

Recall the following standard fact about transversality.

Fact 3.6.4
.

Let
B\xrightarrow{\sigma}A
be smooth maps between manifolds with corners.
Let
W\subset A
be a submanifold.
Suppose
\sigma
is transverse to
W
.

(1)

Then
\sigma^{-1}(W)\subset B
is a submanifold, and the restriction along
\sigma^{-1}(W)\subset B
of the morphism between tangent bundles
\mathsf{T}B\xrightarrow{\mathsf{D}\sigma}\sigma^{\ast}\mathsf{T}A
over
B
descends as an isomorphism between normal bundles over
\sigma^{-1}(W)
:

\nu_{\sigma^{-1}(W)\subset B}\xrightarrow{\cong}\sigma^{\ast}\nu_{W\subset A}~,

where, through a slight abuse of notation, the vector bundle on the right is
\sigma^{\ast}\nu_{W\subset A}:={\sigma_{|\sigma^{-1}(W)}}^{\ast}\nu_{W\subset A}
, the pullback of
\nu_{W\subset A}
along the restriction
\sigma^{-1}(W)\xrightarrow{\sigma_{|\sigma^{-1}(W)}}W
.

(2)

Let
C\xrightarrow{\tau}B
be a smooth map between manifolds with corners.
Then
\tau
is transverse to
\sigma^{-1}(W)
if and only if
\sigma\circ\tau
is transverse to
W
.
In this case, the resulting diagram among vector bundles over
(\sigma\circ\tau)^{-1}(W)
,

be another morphism in
\mathbf{\Delta}
.
Consider the span among topological spaces:

\widetilde{\Bord}^{k}_{p}(M)\underset{\Bord^{k}_{p}(M)}{\times}\Bord^{k}_{q}(M)\xleftarrow{~\pr~}\widetilde{\Bord}^{k}_{p}(M)\underset{\Bord^{k}_{r}(M)}{\times}\Bord^{k}_{r}(M)\xrightarrow{~\sigma_{\ast}\times M~}\widetilde{\Bord}^{k}_{q}(M)\underset{\Bord^{k}_{q}(M)}{\times}\Bord^{k}_{r}(M)~.

Observe that the target of
\pr^{\ast}\alpha_{\sigma}
agrees with the source of
(\sigma_{\ast}\times M)^{\ast}\alpha_{\tau}
.
Fact
3.6.4
(2) implies

\Bigl((\sigma_{\ast}\times M)^{\ast}\alpha_{\tau}\Bigr)\circ(\pr^{\ast}\alpha_{\sigma})~=~\alpha_{\tau\circ\sigma}~.

In the sense of §
6
, these data are a rank-
k
vector bundle over the topological category
\widetilde{\Un}^{k}(M)
.
This vector bundle is then classified by a functor from the underlying
\infty
-category:

\widetilde{\Un}^{k}(M)\xrightarrow{~\nu^{\sf fib}(M)~}\BO(k)~.

Using that the functor between underlying
\infty
-categories
\widetilde{\Un}^{k}(M)\to\Un^{k}(M)
is a left fibration, the functor
\nu^{\sf fib}(M)

Unwinding the construction of
\nu^{\sf fib}_{\bullet}(M)
, its restriction along
\Bord^{k}_{p}(M)=\Sub^{k}(\Delta^{p}\times M)\subset\Un^{k}(M)
is the functor
\nu^{\sf fib}(\Delta^{p}\times M)
of (
27
).

4.
Parametrized Pontryagin–Thom theory

In this section, we fix
p,n,k\geq 0
, an
(n+k)
-manifold
M
with boundary, and a smooth rank-
k
vector bundle
\xi=(E\to B)
. The following is the key result in this section.

Theorem 4.0.1
.

There is a canonical equivalence between simplicial spaces

\cMap^{\pitchfork}_{\bullet}(M,\Th(\xi))\xrightarrow{~\simeq~}\Bord^{\xi}_{\bullet}(M)

which is functorial with respect to open embeddings in
M
.

Corollary 4.0.2
.

The simplicial spaces
\Bord^{k}_{\bullet}(M)
and
\Bord_{\bullet}^{\xi}(M)
both satisfy the Kan condition.

Proof.

Through the equivalence between simplicial spaces
\Bord_{\bullet}^{\xi}(M)\simeq\cMap_{\bullet}^{\pitchfork}(M,\Th(\xi))
of Theorem
4.0.1
, Lemma
2.4.2
immediately implies
\Bord^{\xi}_{\bullet}(M)
satisfies the Kan condition.
Observation
3.5.8
then implies the simplicial space
\Bord^{k}_{\bullet}(M)
satisfies the Kan condition.
∎

Theorem
4.0.1
enables the first part of our main theorem (Theorem
1.0.1
).
The rest of the proof, involving
\dBord^{\xi}_{\bullet}(M)
, is completed through Corollary
5.3.7
.

Proof of Theorem
1.0.1
assuming Theorem
4.0.1
.

Assume
\xi
is a smooth vector bundle.
The equivalence of the theorem then follows from the following sequences of equivalence between spaces, which we explain below:

\bigl|\Bord^{\xi}_{\bullet}(M)\bigr|~\simeq~\left|\cMap^{\pitchfork}_{\bullet}(M,\Th(\xi))\right|~\simeq~\cMap(M,\Th(\xi))~.

The first equivalence is induced by the equivalence of Theorem
4.0.1
.
The second equivalence is Lemma
2.5.6
.

Next, suppose the base
B
of
\xi
has is homotopy equivalent with the geometric realization of a finite simplicial complex
B^{\prime\prime}
.
Choose a piecewise linear embedding
B^{\prime\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{N}
int a large Euclidean space.
A sufficiently small open regular neighborhood of this embedding is a smooth manifold
B^{\prime}
with the same homotopy type:
B\simeq B^{\prime\prime}\simeq B^{\prime}
.
Further, by smooth approximation, the pullback of
\xi
to
B^{\prime}
is isomorphic with a smooth vector bundle
\xi^{\prime}
over the manifold
B^{\prime}
.
The argument above then gives an equivalence
|\Bord^{\xi^{\prime}}_{\bullet}(M)|\simeq\cMap(M,\Th(\xi^{\prime}))
. However, we have evident natural equivalences
|\Bord^{\xi^{\prime}}_{\bullet}(M)|\simeq|\Bord^{\xi}_{\bullet}(M)|
and
\cMap(M,\Th(\xi^{\prime}))\simeq\cMap(M,\Th(\xi))
, from which the result follows.

Lastly, for the general case of
\xi
, its base may be witnessed as a filtered colimit of geometric realizations of finite simplicial complexes:
\colim_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\xi_{i}\simeq\xi
.
Compactness of the submanifolds of
\Delta^{\bullet}\times M
ensures the canonical map between simplicial spaces
\colim_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\Bord^{\xi_{i}}_{\bullet}(M)\to\Bord^{\xi}_{\bullet}(M)
is an equivalence.
Compact support ensures the canonical map between spaces
\colim_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\cMap(M,\Th(\xi_{i}))\to\cMap(M,\Th(\xi))
is an equivalence.
Using that geometric realization commutes with filtered colimits, this general case then follows from the previous case.

∎

4.1.
Parametrized transverse intersection

In this subsection, we fix a
(p+n+k)
-manifold
C
with corners.
In this section, we construct a
family
of manifolds over
\cMap^{\pitchfork}(C,\Th(\xi))
given by taking preimages of the zero-section.

Consider the topological subspace

\widetilde{\cMap}^{\pitchfork}\bigl(C,\Th(\xi)\bigr)~:=~\Bigl\{(f,x)\mid f(x)\in B\Bigr\}~\subset~\cMap^{\pitchfork}(C,\Th(\xi))\times C~.

Projection onto the first coordinate defines a map

(33)

\widetilde{\cMap}^{\pitchfork}(C,\Th(\xi))\xrightarrow{~\pi~}\cMap^{\pitchfork}(C,\Th(\xi))~.

Observation 4.1.1
.

For each
f\in\cMap^{\pitchfork}(C,\Th(\xi))
, there is an identity

\pi^{-1}(f)~=~f^{-1}(B)~\subset~C~.

The following result is the technical underpinning of this article.
It makes specific use of the topologies on
\cMap^{\pitchfork}(C,\Th(\xi))
and
\Sub^{k}(C)
.
Its proof comprises §
4.2
.

Lemma 4.1.2
.

Taking the preimage of the zero-section
B\subset\Th(\xi)
defines a map

(34)

(-)^{-1}(B)\colon\cMap^{\pitchfork}\bigl(C,\Th(\xi)\bigr)\longrightarrow\Sub^{k}(C)~,

\Bigl(C\xrightarrow{f}\Th(\xi)\Bigr)\longmapsto\Bigl(f^{-1}(B)\subset C\Bigr)~.

Observation 4.1.3
.

Through Observation
4.1.1
there is a canonical identification of
\widetilde{\cMap}^{\pitchfork}(C,\Th(\xi))
as a base-change among topological spaces:

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{4.15291pt}{-14.547pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{64}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}~.

Indeed, both
\widetilde{\cMap}^{\pitchfork}(C,\Th(\xi))
and the base-change are subspaces of
\cMap^{\pitchfork}(C,\Th(\xi))\times C
, which can readily be check as identical.

Lemma 4.1.4
.

The map
\widetilde{\cMap}^{\pitchfork}(C,\Th(\xi))\xrightarrow{\pi}\cMap^{\pitchfork}(C,\Th(\xi))
is a fiber bundle of compact codimension-
k
submanifolds of
C
.
In particular,
\pi
is locally trivial.

Proof.

Observation
3.4.1
(2) states that the right vertical map is a fiber bundle of compact codimension-
k
submanifolds of
C
.
The result then follows from Observation
4.1.3
, because the base-change of such is again a fiber bundle of compact codimension-
k
submanifolds of
C
.

∎

Remark 4.1.5
.

After Lemma
4.1.4
, we may interpret the map
\pi
as a
\cMap^{\pitchfork}(C,\Th(\xi))
-family of compact codimension-
k
submanifolds of
C
.

Lemma 4.1.6
.

Taking the preimage of the zero-section
B\subset\Th(\xi)
defines a map between spaces

(35)

(-)^{-1}(B)\colon\cMap^{\pitchfork}\bigl(C,\Th(\xi)\bigr)\longrightarrow\Sub^{\xi}(C)~,

\Bigl(C\xrightarrow{f}\Th(\xi)\Bigr)\longmapsto\Bigl(C\supset f^{-1}(B)\xrightarrow{f_{|}}B\xrightarrow{\xi}\BO(k)\Bigr)~.

Proof.

The composite map

\cMap^{\pitchfork}(C,\Th(\xi))\xrightarrow{~(-)^{-1}(B)~}\Sub^{k}(C)\xrightarrow{~\nu^{\pi}~}\underset{[W]}{\coprod}\Map(W,\BO(k))_{/\Diff(W)}

classifies the fiber bundle
\widetilde{\cMap}^{\pitchfork}(C,\Th(\xi))\xrightarrow{\pi}\cMap^{\pitchfork}(C,\Th(\xi))
of Lemma
4.1.4
equipped with the map

\widetilde{\cMap}^{\pitchfork}(C,\Th(\xi))\xrightarrow{~\nu^{\pi}~}\BO(k)

classifying the rank-
k
vector bundle
\nu^{\pi}
over
\widetilde{\cMap}^{\pitchfork}(C,\Th(\xi))
that is the fiberwise (with respect to Lemma
4.1.4
)
\cMap^{\pitchfork}(C,\Th(\xi))
) normal bundle of
\widetilde{\cMap}^{\pitchfork}(C,\Th(\xi))\subset\cMap^{\pitchfork}(C,\Th(\xi))\times C
.

Consider the evaluation map

(36)

\ev\colon\widetilde{\cMap}^{\pitchfork}(C,\Th(\xi))\longrightarrow B~,\qquad(f,x)\longmapsto f(x)~.

After Lemma
4.1.4
,
this map is classified by a map between spaces

(37)

\cMap^{\pitchfork}(C,\Th(\xi))\longrightarrow\underset{[W]}{\coprod}\Map(W,B)_{/\Diff(W)}~.

Observe the canonical identification between vector bundles
\nu_{B\subset E}\cong\xi
from the normal bundle of the zero-section
B\subset E
.
Using that normal bundles pullback along transverse maps, we have a canonical isomorphism between vector bundles over
\widetilde{\cMap}^{\pitchfork}(C,\Th(\xi))
:

(38)

\nu^{\pi}~\cong~\ev^{\ast}\nu_{B\subset E}~\cong~\ev^{\ast}\xi~.

This canonical isomorphism is a canonical 2-cell witnessing a commutative diagram among spaces

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}~.

By Definition
3.4.3
of
\Sub^{\xi}(C)
as a pullback, this commutative diagram among spaces defines a map between spaces,

\cMap^{\pitchfork}(C,\Th(\xi))\longrightarrow\Sub^{\xi}(C)~.

∎

4.2.
Proof of Lemma
4.1.2

Choose
f\in\cMap^{\pitchfork}(C,\Th(\xi))
.
That is,
f
is a compactly-supported map
C\rightarrow\Th(\xi)
that is smooth over
E\subset\Th(\xi)
and transverse to the zero-section
B\subset E
.
Transversality implies the preimage
f^{-1}(B)\subset C
is a submanifold: because the codimension of
B\subset E\underset{\rm opn}{\subset}\Th(\xi)
is
k
, the codimension of
f^{-1}(B)\subset C
is also
k
.
Because the zero-section
B\subset\Th(\xi)
can be separated from the base-point of
\Th(\xi)
by disjoint open subsets, then
f^{-1}(B)\subset{\sf Supp}(f)
.
Consequently, compactness of the support of
f
implies compactness of
f^{-1}(B)
.
Therefore, we have
f^{-1}(B)\in\Sub^{k}(C)
.
Consequently, the assignment
f\mapsto f^{-1}(B)
is a well-defined function between sets (
34
).

In the remainder of this proof, we show (
34
) is continuous at each point in
\cMap^{\pitchfork}\bigl(C,\Th(\xi)\bigr)
. That is, for each point

\Bigl(C\xrightarrow{f}\Th(\xi)\Bigr)\in\cMap^{\pitchfork}\bigl(C,\Th(\xi)\bigr)

there exists an open neighborhood
V
of
f
for which the composite map

V\xrightarrow{~\rm inclusion~}\cMap^{\pitchfork}\bigl(C,\Th(\xi)\bigr)\xrightarrow{(-)^{-1}(B)}\Sub^{k}(C)

is continuous. We do this by choosing
V
sufficiently small that we can construct a function
\Phi
fitting into a commutative diagram among sets

(39)

\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi}
\scriptstyle{\rm inclusion}
\textstyle{\Emb\bigl(f^{-1}(B),C\bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sf Image}
\textstyle{\cMap^{\pitchfork}\bigl(C,\Th(\xi)\bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(-)^{-1}(B)}
\textstyle{\Sub^{k}(C)}

and then establishing continuity of
\Phi
.
We implement this through the following
Steps
:

(1)

Using that the topology on
\cMap^{\pitchfork}\bigl(C,\Th(\xi)\bigr)
is finer than the compact-open topology, we construct an open neighborhood

f~\in~V^{\prime}~\subset~\cMap^{\pitchfork}\bigl(C,\Th(\xi)\bigr)

with the following property.

There is a closed neighborhood
B\subset\overline{U}\subset E
such that the map given by restriction along
f^{-1}(\overline{U})
factors:

(40)

\textstyle{V^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rm inclusion}
\textstyle{\cMap^{\pitchfork}\bigl(C,\Th(\xi)\bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rm restriction}
\textstyle{\Map^{\sf sm}(f^{-1}(\overline{U}),E)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rm inclusion}
\textstyle{\Map^{\sf sm}\bigl(f^{-1}(\overline{U}),\Th(\xi)\bigr).}

(2)

Using the compact-open
\mathsf{C}^{\infty}
topology of
\Map^{\sf sm}(f^{-1}(\overline{U}),E)
, we identify an open neighborhood of
f_{|f^{-1}(\overline{U})}\in\Map^{\sf sm}(f^{-1}(\overline{U}),E)
with a convex open neighborhood of the origin

\Gamma^{\tau}~\subset~\Gamma\Bigl((f_{|f^{-1}(\overline{U})})^{\ast}\mathsf{T}E\to f^{-1}(\overline{U})\Bigr)

in the topological vector space of smooth sections of the pullback of the tangent bundle of
E
along
f_{|f^{-1}(\overline{U})}
.
We define the smaller open neighborhood

f~\in~V~\subset~V^{\prime}

as the preimage of
\Gamma^{\tau}
along the dashed map of the previous point.

(3)

Using the convexity of
\Gamma^{\tau}
, we construct a homeomorphism over
V
,

\varphi\colon V\times f^{-1}(B)~\cong~\pi^{-1}(V)=\widetilde{\cMap}^{\pitchfork}(C,\Th(\xi))_{|V}~,

which is smooth in an appropriate sense.

(4)

Using smoothness of
\varphi
, the adjoint of the composite map

V\times f^{-1}(B)\xrightarrow{\varphi}\pi^{-1}(V)\hookrightarrow\cMap^{\pitchfork}\bigl(C,\Th(\xi)\bigr)\times C\xrightarrow{\pr}C

defines a map

\Phi\colon V\longrightarrow\Emb\bigl(f^{-1}(B),C\bigr)~.

We finish by observing that this map
\Phi
fits into the commutative diagram (
39
).

Step (
1
)
: Once and for all, choose a complete Riemannian metric on
B
, and choose an inner-product on the vector bundle
\xi=(E\to B)
.
Together, these structures determine a complete Riemannian metric on
E
.
Through an application of the inverse function theorem, choose a smooth map
E\xrightarrow{\epsilon}\mathbb{R}_{>0}
such that, for each
p\in E
, the open ball
\mathsf{B}_{\epsilon(p)}(p)\subset E
is geodesically convex.
For
B\xrightarrow{\delta}\mathbb{R}_{>0}
a smooth map, denote the
\delta
-neighborhood of
B\subset E
as

U_{<\delta}~:=~\{e\in E\mid\exists b\in B\text{ for which }d(b,e)<\delta(b)\}\subset E~.

Denote by
U_{\leq\delta}
similarly, replacing the relation
<
by
\leq
.

For any
\delta<\epsilon
sufficiently small,
U_{<\delta}
is a tubular neighborhood of
B\subset E
.
Using that
f
is transverse to
B
, there exists a sufficiently small such
\delta
such that, for each
0<\delta^{\prime}\leq\delta
, the preimage
f^{-1}(U_{<\delta^{\prime}})
is a tubular neighborhood of
f^{-1}(B)\subset C
.
Choose such a sufficiently small
\delta
.

Using that
f
is compactly-supported, the preimage
f^{-1}(B)\subset C
is compact.
It follows that any closed tubular neighborhood of it is compact.
In particular, for each
0<\delta^{\prime}<\delta
, the preimage
f^{-1}(U_{\leq\delta^{\prime}})\subset C
is compact.

Consider the following subset of
\cMap^{\pitchfork}\bigl(C,\Th(\xi)\bigr)
:

V^{\prime}~:=~\left\{g\mid C^{+}\smallsetminus f^{-1}(U_{<2\delta/5})\subset g^{-1}\Bigl(\Th(\xi)\smallsetminus U_{\leq\delta/5}\Bigr)\text{ and }f^{-1}(U_{\leq 3\delta/5})\subset g^{-1}(U_{<4\delta/5})\right\}~.

Note that
f\in V^{\prime}
.
Recall that
f^{-1}(U_{\leq 2\delta/5})
and
f^{-1}(U_{\leq 4\delta/5})
are compact.
Because the topology on
\cMap^{\pitchfork}\bigl(C,\Th(\xi)\bigr)
is finer than the compact-open topology, this subset
V^{\prime}\subset\cMap^{\pitchfork}\bigl(C,\Th(\xi)\bigr)
is open.

We record two important observations about an element
g\in V^{\prime}
.
First, the assumed containment
C^{+}\smallsetminus f^{-1}(U_{<2\delta/5})\subset g^{-1}\Bigl(\Th(\xi)\smallsetminus U_{\leq\delta/5}\Bigr)
implies the containment
g^{-1}(U_{<\delta/5})\subset f^{-1}(U_{<2\delta/5})
.
Therefore,
g(x)\in B
implies
x\in f^{-1}(U_{<2\delta/5})
.
Consequently, the containment
(g_{|f^{-1}(U_{\leq 2\delta/5})})^{-1}(B)\subset g^{-1}(B)

is entire:

(41)

(g_{|f^{-1}(U_{\leq 2\delta/5})})^{-1}(B)~=~g^{-1}(B)~.

Second, the assumed containment

f^{-1}(U_{\leq 3\delta/5})\subset g^{-1}(U_{<4\delta/5})

implies restriction along the subspace
f^{-1}(U_{\leq 2\delta/5})\subset C
defines a map

(42)

V^{\prime}\xrightarrow{~(-)_{|f^{-1}(U_{\leq 2\delta/5})}}\Map^{\sm}\bigl(f^{-1}(U_{\leq 2\delta/5}),E\bigr)~.

as in (
40
). Setting
\overline{U}:=U_{\leq 2\delta/5}
, observe that this completes Step (
1
).

Step (
2
)
: Henceforth, we denote

N~:=~f^{-1}(U_{\leq\delta/5})~,

which is a compact manifold with boundary, and a closed tubular neighborhood of
f^{-1}(B)
.

Consider the exponential map

\mathsf{T}E\xrightarrow{~{\sf exp}~}E~.

Consider the open subset of the tangent bundle of
E
:

\mathsf{T}^{\epsilon}E~:=~\left\{(e,v)\mid\lVert v\rVert<\epsilon(e)\right\}~\subset~\mathsf{T}E~.

Denote the composite map

{\sf exp}^{\epsilon}\colon\mathsf{T}^{\epsilon}E\hookrightarrow\mathsf{T}E\xrightarrow{~{\sf exp}~}E~.

Observe the commutative diagram among topological spaces

\textstyle{(f_{|N})^{\ast}\mathsf{T}^{\epsilon}E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pr}
\textstyle{\mathsf{T}^{\epsilon}E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\sf exp}^{\epsilon}}
\textstyle{N\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rm zero}
\scriptstyle{f_{|N}}
\textstyle{E.}

Now, the choice of
\epsilon
is such that
{\sf exp}^{\epsilon}
is a surjective submersion.
In particular,
{\sf exp}^{\epsilon}
is transverse to
B\subset E
, and the preimage
({\sf exp}^{\epsilon})^{-1}(B)\subset\mathsf{T}^{\epsilon}E
is a smooth submanifold.
By definition of
f
, the bottom horizontal map
f_{|N}
is transverse to
B
.
Therefore, the composite map
N\xrightarrow{\pr\circ{\rm zero}}\mathsf{T}^{\epsilon}E
is transverse to
({\sf exp}^{\epsilon})^{-1}(B)\subset\mathsf{T}^{\epsilon}E
.
In other words, the map
\pr
is transverse to
({\sf exp}^{\epsilon})^{-1}(B)
along the zero-section.
Consequently, the map
\pr
is transverse to
({\sf exp}^{\epsilon})^{-1}(B)
along a tubular neighborhood of the zero-section.
Let us make the previous sentence more specific.

For each smooth map
N\xrightarrow{\rho}\mathbb{R}_{>0}
satisfying
\rho(x)<\epsilon(f(x))
for all
x\in N
, consider the open subspace

\Bigl((f_{|N})^{\ast}\mathsf{T}^{\epsilon}E\Bigr)^{\rho}~:=~\left\{(x,v)\mid\lVert v\rVert<\rho(x)\right\}~\subset~(f_{|N})^{\ast}\mathsf{T}^{\epsilon}E~.

There exists such a
\rho
such that the composite map

\pr^{\rho}\colon\Bigl((f_{|N})^{\ast}\mathsf{T}^{\epsilon}E\Bigr)^{\rho}~\hookrightarrow~(f_{|N})^{\ast}\mathsf{T}^{\epsilon}E\xrightarrow{~\pr~}\mathsf{T}^{\epsilon}E

is transverse to
({\sf exp}^{\epsilon})^{-1}(B)\subset\mathsf{T}^{\epsilon}B
.

Choose such a
\rho
.
In particular, the preimage

({\sf exp}^{\epsilon}\circ\pr^{\rho})^{-1}(B)~=~(\pr^{\rho})^{-1}\Bigl(\Bigl({\sf exp}^{\epsilon}\Bigr)^{-1}(B)\Bigr)\subset\Bigl((f_{|N})^{\ast}\mathsf{T}^{\epsilon}E\Bigr)^{\rho}

is a smooth submanifold.

Value-wise vector addition and value-wise scaling defines a topological vector space structure on the space of smooth sections,

\Gamma^{\sm}\left(\left(f_{|N}\right)^{\ast}\mathsf{T}E\rightarrow N\right)~,

endowed with the compact-open
\mathsf{C}^{\infty}
topology.
This topological vector space is locally convex.
Post-composition with the open embedding
\Bigl((f_{|N})^{\ast}\mathsf{T}^{\epsilon}E\Bigr)^{\rho}\hookrightarrow(f_{|N})^{\ast}\mathsf{T}^{\epsilon}E
defines an open embedding

\Gamma^{\sm}\left(\left(\left(f_{|N}\right)^{\ast}\mathsf{T}^{\epsilon}E\right)^{\rho}\rightarrow N\right)~\hookrightarrow~\Gamma^{\sm}\left(\left(f_{|N}\right)^{\ast}\mathsf{T}E\rightarrow N\right)~.

This open embedding contains the zero-section.
Furthermore, post-composition with
\pr^{\rho}
and
{\sf exp}^{\epsilon}
define a map

\Gamma^{\sm}\left(\left(\left(f_{|N}\right)^{\ast}\mathsf{T}^{\epsilon}E\right)^{\rho}\rightarrow N\right)\xrightarrow{~{\sf exp}^{\epsilon}\circ\pr^{\rho}\circ-~}\Map^{\sm}\left(N,E\right)~.

The choice of
\epsilon
ensures this map is an open embedding.

Consider the subset of
\Gamma^{\sm}\left(\left(\left(f_{|N}\right)^{\ast}\mathsf{T}^{\epsilon}E\right)^{\rho}\rightarrow N\right)
,

(43)

\Gamma^{\pitchfork}\left(\left(\left(f_{|N}\right)^{\ast}\mathsf{T}^{\epsilon}E\right)^{\rho}\rightarrow N\right)~:=~\Bigl\{\sigma\mid\sigma\pitchfork({\sf exp}^{\epsilon}\circ\pr^{\rho})^{-1}(B)\Bigr\}~,

consisting of those smooth sections that are transverse to the submanifold
({\sf exp}^{\epsilon}\circ\pr^{\rho})^{-1}(B)\subset\Bigl((f_{|N})^{\ast}\mathsf{T}^{\epsilon}E\Bigr)^{\rho}
.
Using that
N
is compact,
Theorem 2.1 of
[
Hi
]
applies to conclude that this subset (
43
) is open.
In particular, because this subset (
43
) contains the zero-section, and using that these spaces of smooth sections are locally convex, there is a convex open neighborhood of the zero-section,

\Gamma^{\tau}~\subset~\Gamma^{\sm}\left(\left(\left(f_{|N}\right)^{\ast}\mathsf{T}^{\epsilon}E\right)^{\rho}\rightarrow N\right)~,

such that each
\sigma\in\Gamma^{\tau}
is transverse to

({\sf exp}^{\epsilon}\circ\pr^{\rho})^{-1}(B)\subset\Bigl((f_{|N})^{\ast}\mathsf{T}^{\epsilon}E\Bigr)^{\rho}
.
Consider the pullback among topological spaces:

(44)

\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{V^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\ref{e21})}
\textstyle{\Gamma^{\tau}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\sf exp}^{\epsilon}\circ\pr^{\rho}\circ-}
\textstyle{\Map^{\sm}\bigl(N,E\bigr)~.}

Continuity of the map (
42
) implies
V\subset V^{\prime}
is open.
Openness of
V^{\prime}\subset\cMap^{\pitchfork}\bigl(C,\Th(\xi)\bigr)
implies
V\subset\cMap^{\pitchfork}\bigl(C,\Th(\xi)\bigr)
is open.
Because
\Gamma^{\tau}
contains the zero-section, there is membership
f\in V
. This completes Step (
2
).

Step (
3
)
: Consider the pullback

Using that
\Gamma^{\tau}
is convex, straight-line homotopy to the zero-section defines a map

I\times\Gamma^{\tau}\xrightarrow{~\rm scale~}\Gamma^{\tau}~,\qquad(t,\sigma)\longmapsto t\sigma~,

where
I:=[0,1]\subset\mathbb{R}
is the closed interval.
