Once and for all, choose a complete Riemannian metric on
C
.
With the standard Riemannian metrics on
I
, this determines a complete Riemannian metric on
I\times C
.
Consider the vector bundle
\Gamma^{\tau}\times\mathsf{T}(I\times C)
over
\Gamma^{\tau}\times I\times C
.
Using that for each
\sigma\in\Gamma^{\tau}
the subspace
(\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\tau}_{I})_{|\{\sigma\}}\subset\{\sigma\}\times I\times C
is a compact submanifold with corners,
consider the subspace of its base-change over
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\tau}_{I}\subset\Gamma^{\tau}\times I\times C
,

\mathsf{T}^{\sf fib}\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\tau}_{I}~:=~\left\{(\sigma,(x,v))\mid v\in\mathsf{T}_{x}(\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\tau}_{I})_{|\{\sigma\}}\right\}~\subset~\Bigl(\Gamma^{\tau}\times\mathsf{T}(I\times C)\Bigr)_{|\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\tau}_{I}}~.

Orthogonal projection with respect to the given Riemannian metric on
I\times C
defines a surjection over
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\tau}_{I}
:

(47)

{\sf pr}_{\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\tau}_{I}}\colon\Bigl(\Gamma^{\tau}\times\mathsf{T}(I\times C)\Bigr)_{|\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\tau}_{I}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{T}^{\sf fib}\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\tau}_{I}~.

The standard coordinate vector field on the closed interval
I
defines a continuous section
T
of the vector bundle
\Gamma^{\tau}\times\mathsf{T}(I\times C)
over
\Gamma^{\tau}\times I\times C
, which, for each
\sigma\in\Gamma^{\tau}
, restricts as a smooth section of the vector bundle
\{\sigma\}\times\mathsf{T}(I\times C)\to\{\sigma\}\times I\times C
.
Flow by this section implements translation in the
I
-coordinate of
\Gamma^{\tau}\times I\times C
.
The restriction of
T
over
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\tau}_{I}
projects via (
47
) to a section
{\sf pr}_{\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\tau}_{I}}(T)
of the map
\mathsf{T}^{\sf fib}\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\tau}_{I}\to\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\tau}_{I}
.
Now, for each
\sigma\in\Gamma^{\tau}
, consider the map:

(\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\tau}_{I})_{|\{\sigma\}}\longrightarrow I\times\{\sigma\}=I~.

By construction of
\Gamma^{\tau}
, this map is a proper smooth map with no critical points.
Consequently, the section
{\sf pr}_{\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\tau}_{I}}(T)
is nowhere vanishing and gradient-like.
For each
\sigma\in\Gamma^{\tau}
, flow by this section defines a diffeomorphism between manifolds with corners:

\psi_{\sigma}\colon\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\tau}_{|\{\sigma\}}\underset{(\ref{e27})}{=}(\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\tau}_{I})_{|\{\sigma\}\times\{1\}}\xrightarrow{~\cong~}(\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\tau}_{I})_{|\{\sigma\}\times\{0\}}\underset{(\ref{e27})}{=}f^{-1}(B)~.

These diffeomorphisms assemble as a function between sets over
\Gamma^{\tau}
:

(48)

\psi\colon\Gamma^{\tau}\times f^{-1}(B)\xrightarrow{~\cong~}\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\tau}~,\qquad(\sigma,x)\longmapsto\psi_{\sigma}(x)~,

which restricts to each fiber as a diffeomorphism between compact manifolds with corners.
Using smooth dependence on initial conditions of solutions to ODE (e.g., Chapter XIV of
[
La
]
), this map (
48
) is a homeomorphism.
Through the base-change identity (
45
), this homeomorphism base-changes as a homeomorphism

(49)

\varphi\colon V\times f^{-1}(B)\xrightarrow{~\cong~}\widetilde{\cMap}^{\pitchfork}(C,\Th(\xi))_{|V}~,\qquad(g,x)\longmapsto\phi_{g}(x)~,

over
V
, which restricts to each fiber as a diffeomorphism between compact submanifolds of
C
. This completes Step (
3
).

Step (
4
)
: This homeomorphism
\varphi
is adjoint to a function

(50)

\Phi\colon V\longrightarrow\Emb\bigl(f^{-1}(B),C\bigr)~,\qquad g\longmapsto\Bigl(x\longmapsto\phi_{g}(x)\Bigr)~.

Again by smooth dependence on initial conditions of solutions to ODE, this function is continuous.
Observe that the diagram of functions between topological spaces

\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi}
\scriptstyle{\rm inclusion}
\textstyle{\Emb\bigl(f^{-1}(B),C\bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sf Image}
\textstyle{\cMap^{\pitchfork}\bigl(C,\Th(\xi)\bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(-)^{-1}(B)}
\textstyle{\Sub^{k}(C)}

commutes.
Continuity of
\Phi
and
{\sf Image}
implies the restriction of the function
(-)^{-1}(B)
along the open subset
V\subset\cMap^{\pitchfork}\bigl(C,\Th(\xi)\bigr)
is continuous.
In particular, the function
(-)^{-1}(B)
is continuous at
f\in\cMap^{\pitchfork}\bigl(C,\Th(\xi)\bigr)
, which completes Step (
4
) and the proof as desired.

4.3.
Parametrized collapse maps

Fix a
(p+n+k)
-manifold
C
with corners.
In this section, we use parametrized collapse maps to construct a homotopy inverse to the map

\cMap^{\pitchfork}(C,\Th(\xi))\xrightarrow{~(-)^{-1}(B)~}\Sub^{\xi}(C)

of Lemma
4.1.2
, at least on compact families.

Remark 4.3.1
.

We outline a construction of an inverse,

\Sub^{\xi}(C)\longrightarrow\cMap^{\pitchfork}(C,\Th(\xi))~,

supposing the space
\Sub^{\xi}(C)
admits a natural structure of an infinite-dimensional smooth manifold.
Under this supposition, the subspace

\widetilde{\Sub}^{\xi}(C)\subset\Sub^{\xi}(C)\times C

projects to
\Sub^{\xi}(C)
as a smooth fiber bundle of compact submanifolds of
C
.
Also, it is equipped with a map

\widetilde{\Sub}^{\xi}(C)\xrightarrow{~g~}B~.

For
\widetilde{\nu}^{\sf fib}(C)
the fiberwise (over
\Sub^{\xi}(C)
) normal bundle, there is a canonical isomorphism between vector bundles over
\widetilde{\Sub}^{\xi}(C)
:

\alpha\colon\widetilde{\nu}^{\sf fib}(C)~\cong~g^{\ast}\xi~.

Invoking a tubular neighborhood theorem for finite-codimensional submanifolds of infinite-dimensional manifolds,
choose a closed tubular neighborhood

\widetilde{\Sub}^{\xi}(C)~\subset~\overline{N}~\subset~\Sub^{\xi}(C)\times C

and denote its interior as
N
;
choose, a homeomorphism

N~\overset{\varphi}{\cong}~E(\nu^{\sf fib}(C))

with the total space of the fiberwise normal bundle.
Such a choice of
\varphi
would determine a composite map

\Sub^{\xi}(C)\times C\xrightarrow{~\rm collapse~}\frac{\overline{N}}{\partial\overline{N}}\xrightarrow{~\varphi^{+}~}\Th(\nu^{\sf fib}(C))\xrightarrow{~\alpha~}\Th(h^{\ast}\xi)\longrightarrow\Th(\xi)

which is adjoint to the desired homotopy inverse.
It should then be straightforward to check this map is a section to the map
(-)^{-1}(B)
.
To see that
(-)^{-1}(B)
is a homotopy section would lastly follow using a family of maps
(0,\infty]\times\Th(\xi)\xrightarrow{\Phi}\Th(\xi)
such that the support of
\Phi_{t}
is a closed
t
-neighborhood of the zero-section. Our proof of Theorem
4.3.2
follows this outline over compact finite-dimensional smooth families (in fact, over disks), where smooth manifold theory is well-established.

Theorem 4.3.2
.

The map (
35
) is an equivalence between spaces:

(-)^{-1}(B)\colon\cMap^{\pitchfork}(C,\Th(\xi))\xrightarrow{~\simeq~}\Sub^{\xi}(C)~.

Proof.

We prove that the relative homotopy groups of the map vanish.
Fix
q\geq 0
, and consider a solid commutative diagram among spaces in which the vertical maps are as named:

(51)

\textstyle{\mathbb{S}^{q-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rm inclusion}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{f}_{0}}
\textstyle{\cMap^{\pitchfork}(C,\Th(\xi))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(-)^{-1}(B)}
\textstyle{\mathbb{D}^{q}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{f}}
\textstyle{\Sub^{\xi}(C).}

We wish to construct a filler
\widetilde{f}
among spaces.

Note that we may choose a convenient representative of the homotopy classes of the pair of maps
(f,\widetilde{f}_{0})
.

•

The map
\widetilde{f}_{0}
is adjoint to a compactly-supported map
\mathbb{S}^{q-1}\times C\xrightarrow{\widetilde{f}_{0}}\Th(\xi)
that restricts over
E
to each fiber over
\mathbb{S}^{q-1}
as a smooth map transverse to the zero-section
B\subset E
.
By smooth approximation, and genericity of transversality, we may homotope
\widetilde{f}_{0}
to assume it is adjoint to a map that is smooth over
E
and transverse to
B\subset E
.

•

Denote the composite map

W_{f}\colon\mathbb{D}^{q}\xrightarrow{~f~}\Sub^{\xi}(C)\longrightarrow\Sub^{k}(C)~.

Consider the topological subspace

\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{q}~:=~W_{f}^{\ast}\widetilde{\Sub}^{k}(C)~=~\left\{(x,c)\mid c\in W_{f}(x)\right\}~\subset~\mathbb{D}^{q}\times C~,

which is the base-change of
\widetilde{\Sub}^{k}(C)\to\Sub^{k}(C)
along
W_{f}
.
By smooth approximation, we may homotope
f
relative to
\mathbb{S}^{q-1}\subset\mathbb{D}^{q}
to ensure
\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{q}\subset\mathbb{D}^{q}\times C
is a smooth submanifold.
Observation
3.4.1
(2) ensures the projection

\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{q}\xrightarrow{~\pi~}\mathbb{D}^{q}

is a smooth fiber bundle.
Recall that the map
f
supplies a map
g\colon\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{q}\rightarrow B
as well as an isomomorphism between vector bundles

\alpha\colon\nu^{\pi}~\cong~g^{\ast}\xi

from the vertical normal bundle of
\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{q}\xrightarrow{\pi}\mathbb{D}^{q}
to the pullback of
\xi
.
By smooth approximation,
we may homotope
g
and
\alpha
to assume
g
is a smooth map and
\alpha
is an isomorphism between smooth vector bundles.

Once and for all, choose a smooth inner-product on the smooth vector bundle
\xi
.
For each
\delta>0
, denote the subspaces
U_{<\delta}\subset U_{\leq\delta}\subset E
consisting of those elements whose norm is, respectively, less than
\delta
and no more than
\delta
.
Once and for all, choose a smooth map

\sigma\colon(0,\infty]\times\mathbb{R}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}

with the following properties.

•

There is an open neighborhood
(0,\infty]\times\{0\}\subset O\subset(0,\infty]\times\mathbb{R}
such that
\sigma(t,x)=(t,x)
for each
(t,x)\in O
.

•

For each
t\in(0,\infty]
, the smooth map
\sigma_{t}:=\sigma_{|\{t\}\times\mathbb{R}}\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
is an open embedding with image
\mathbb{B}_{t}(0)\subset\mathbb{R}
, the open ball of radius
t
about the origin.

•

The map
\sigma_{\infty}=\id_{\mathbb{R}}
is the identity.

Consider the map

(0,\infty]\times\Th(\xi)\xrightarrow{~\Phi~}\Th(\xi)~,\qquad(t,e)\longmapsto\begin{cases}e&,~\lVert e\rVert=0\\
\sigma_{t}^{-1}(\lVert e\rVert)\cdot\frac{e}{\lVert e\rVert}&,~0<\lVert e\rVert<t\\
+&,~t\leq\lVert e\rVert\end{cases}~.

Observe that
\Phi
is continuous, and is smooth over
E
.

Using that the compactly-supported map
\mathbb{S}^{q-1}\times C\xrightarrow{\widetilde{f}_{0}}\Th(\xi)
is smooth over
E
and transverse to
B\subset E
, transversality ensures the existence of a
\delta>0
such that each
0<\delta^{\prime}<\delta
has the following properties.

•

\widetilde{f}_{0}^{-1}(U_{<\delta^{\prime}})\subset\mathbb{S}^{q-1}\times C
is a tubular neighborhood of
\widetilde{f}_{0}^{-1}(B)\subset\mathbb{S}^{q-1}\times C
.

•

The subspace
\widetilde{f}_{0}^{-1}(U_{<\delta^{\prime}})\subset\mathbb{S}^{q-1}\times C
is a smooth fiber subbundle with respect to the projection to
\mathbb{S}^{q-1}
.

Denote the map

\widetilde{f}_{0|}\colon\widetilde{f}_{0}^{-1}(B)\longrightarrow B~,\qquad(u,c)\longmapsto\widetilde{f}_{0}(u,c)~.

Denote the open subset

N_{0}~:=~\widetilde{f}_{0}^{-1}(U_{<\delta/2})~\subset~\mathbb{S}^{q-1}\times C~.

Observe the pointed continuous factorization, which is necessarily unique, through the collapse map:

(52)

\hbox to149pt{\vbox to57.67pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 77.06952pt\lower-26.87062pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

\lxSVG@closescope }}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{0.46574pt}{21.42363pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{64}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{52.91574pt}{-1.41724pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{64}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope {}{

{{{{{}}{
{}{}}{}{}{{}{}}}}}{}{{{{{}}{

{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.94424pt}{-14.35812pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{64}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}~.

Observe the canonical identification between vector bundles
\nu_{B\subset E}\cong\xi
from the normal bundle of the zero-section
B\subset E
.
Consider the normal bundle
\nu_{\widetilde{f}_{0}}
of the submanifold
\widetilde{f}_{0}^{-1}(B)\subset\mathbb{S}^{q-1}\times C
.
Using that normal bundles pullback along transverse maps (Fact
3.6.4
(1)), we have a canonical isomorphism between smooth vector bundles over
\widetilde{f}_{0}^{-1}(B)
:

\nu_{\widetilde{f}_{0}}~\cong~\widetilde{f}_{0|}^{\ast}\nu_{B\subset E}~\cong~\widetilde{f}_{0|}^{\ast}\xi~.

This isomorphism determines a pointed map

(53)

\Th(\nu_{\widetilde{f}_{0}})\cong\Th(\widetilde{f}_{0|}^{\ast}\xi)\longrightarrow\Th(\xi)~.

Using the tubular neighborhood theorem, choose a diffeomorphism between the total space of the normal bundle
\nu_{\widetilde{f}_{0}}
and the tubular neighborhood
N_{0}
of
\widetilde{f}_{0}^{-1}(B)\subset\mathbb{S}^{q-1}\times C
,

E(\nu_{\widetilde{f}_{0}})\overset{\varphi_{0}}{~\cong~}N_{0}~,

such that the diagram among pointed topological spaces

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{N_{0}^{+}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Th(\xi)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Th(\nu_{\widetilde{f}_{0}})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{f}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{0}^{+}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\ref{e35})}

commutes.

Now, using the tubular neighborhood theorem, choose the following data:

•

A tubular neighborhood
\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{q}\subset N\subset\mathbb{D}^{q}\times M
such that the projection
N\to\mathbb{D}^{q}
is a smooth fiber bundle and there is an identity
N_{0}=N\cap\Bigl(\mathbb{S}^{q-1}\times C\Bigr)
between subsets of
\mathbb{S}^{q-1}\times C
.

•

A diffeomorphism
E(\nu^{\pi})\overset{\varphi}{\cong}N
under
\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{q}
extending
\varphi_{0}
.

Using that
\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{q}
is compact, we can shrink
N
as needed to assume its closure
\overline{N}\subset\mathbb{D}^{q}\times C
is compact.
These data determine the composite map

\widetilde{f}\colon\mathbb{D}^{q}\times C\xrightarrow{\rm collapse}N^{+}\overset{\varphi^{+}}{\cong}\Th(\nu^{\pi})\overset{\Th(\alpha)}{\cong}\Th(g^{\ast}\xi)\to\Th(\xi)~.

Let us denote the adjoint of this map again as
\mathbb{D}^{q}\xrightarrow{\widetilde{f}}\cMap(C,\Th(\xi))
.
Observe that
\widetilde{f}^{-1}(E)=N\subset\mathbb{D}^{q}\times M
.
Because the closure of
N
is compact, this map
\widetilde{f}
is compactly-supported.
For
E(\chi)
to denote the total space of a smooth vector bundle
\chi
, the definition of
\widetilde{f}
is such that the restriction of
\widetilde{f}
to
N
is a composition:

\widetilde{f}_{|N}\colon N\xrightarrow{\varphi}E(\nu^{\pi})\xrightarrow{E(\alpha)}E(g^{\ast}\xi)\xrightarrow{E(g)}E(\xi)=E~.

Because each factor in this composition is smooth, then
\widetilde{f}_{|N}
is smooth.
Because
E(g)
is a fiberwise isomorphism between smooth vector bundles, it is transverse to the zero-section and the preimage of the zero-section is precisely the zero-section.
Because
\varphi
and
\alpha
are diffeomorphisms under
\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{q}
, we conclude that
\widetilde{f}_{|N}
is transverse to the zero-section
B\subset E
, and the preimage
\widetilde{f}^{-1}(B)=\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{q}
.
We conclude that
\widetilde{f}
defines a map as in (
51
) making the lower right triangle commute.

By construction of
\mathbb{D}^{q}\times C\xrightarrow{\widetilde{f}}\Th(\xi)
, its restriction is identical with the map in (
52
):

\widetilde{f}_{|\mathbb{S}^{q-1}\times C}~=~\overline{f}_{0}\circ{\rm collapse}\underset{(\ref{e41})}{~=~}\Phi_{\delta/2}\circ\widetilde{f}_{0}~.

Using that
\sigma_{\infty}=\id
,
the map
[\delta/2,\infty]\times\Th(\xi)\xrightarrow{\Phi}\Th(\xi)
then supplies a path between
\mathbb{S}^{q-1}\xrightarrow{\widetilde{f}_{0}}\cMap^{\pitchfork}(C,\Th(\xi))

and
\mathbb{S}^{q-1}\xrightarrow{\widetilde{f}_{|\mathbb{S}^{q-1}}}\cMap^{\pitchfork}(C,\Th(\xi))
:

\widetilde{f}_{|\mathbb{S}^{q-1}}~\simeq~\widetilde{f}_{0}~.

In other words, the upper left triangle in (
51
) homotopy commutes, thereby completing the proof.

∎

4.4.
Simplicially compatible parametrized transverse intersections

Here, we prove Theorem
4.0.1
.

Lemma 4.4.1
.

The maps

\cMap^{\pitchfork}_{p}(M,\Th(\xi)):=\cMap^{\pitchfork}(\Delta^{p}\times M,\Th(\xi))\xrightarrow{(-)^{-1}(B)}\Sub^{k}(\Delta^{p}\times M)=\Bord^{k}_{p}(M)

of Lemma
4.1.2
respect the simplicial structure morphisms:

\cMap^{\pitchfork}_{\bullet}(M,\Th(\xi))\xrightarrow{~(-)^{-1}(B)~}\Bord^{k}_{\bullet}(M)~.

Proof.

Let
[p]\xrightarrow{\sigma}[q]
be a morphism in
\mathbf{\Delta}
.
We must show the diagram among topological spaces

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\cMap^{\pitchfork}_{q}(M,\Th(\xi))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Bord^{k}_{q}(M)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\cMap^{\pitchfork}_{p}(M,\Th(\xi))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Bord^{k}_{p}(M)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{-1}(B)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{-1}(B)}

commutes.
Let
(\Delta^{q}\times M\xrightarrow{f}\Th(\xi))\in\cMap^{\pitchfork}_{q}(M,\Th(\xi))
.
Commutativity of the diagram follows upon observing the sequence of identities among subsets of
\Delta^{p}\times M
:

\displaystyle(\sigma^{\ast}(f))^{-1}(B)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle(f\circ(\sigma_{\ast}\times M))^{-1}(B)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\sigma_{\ast}\times M)^{-1}\Bigl(f^{-1}(B)\Bigr)

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle\sigma^{\ast}\Bigl(f^{-1}(B)\Bigr)~.

The first identity follows from the definition of the left vertical map
\sigma^{\ast}
.
The second identity is a consequence of the general fact that the preimage of a composition is an iterated preimage.
The last identity is the definition of the right vertical map
\sigma^{\ast}
, applied to
(f^{-1}(B)\subset\Delta^{q}\times M)\in\Bord^{k}_{q}(M)
.

∎

We now construct the morphism

(54)

\cMap^{\pitchfork}_{\bullet}(M,\Th(\xi))\longrightarrow\Bord^{\xi}_{\bullet}(M)~,

which Theorem
4.0.1
states is an equivalence.
In what follows, we reference the material of §
3.6
.
Consider the category-object in
\Top
over
\mathbf{\Delta}
,

\Un^{\pitchfork}(M)\longrightarrow\mathbf{\Delta}~,

that is the Grothendieck construction of the simplicial topological space
\cMap^{\pitchfork}_{\bullet}(M,\Th(\xi))
.
Its target morphism is a covering space, and therefore it is a topological category.
On underlying
\infty
-categories, it is the unstraightening of the simplicial space underlying
\cMap^{\pitchfork}_{\bullet}(M,\Th(\xi))
.
Through the functoriality of the Grothendieck construction, the morphism
\cMap^{\pitchfork}_{\bullet}(M,\Th(\xi))\to\Bord^{k}_{\bullet}(M)
between simplicial topological spaces of Lemma
4.4.1
supplies a morphism over
\mathbf{\Delta}
between topological categories in
\Top
:

\Un^{\pitchfork}(M)\longrightarrow\Un^{k}(M)~.

Denote the pullback among category-objects in
\Top
:

(55)

\lxSVG@closescope {

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}~.

Because the right vertical morphism is a left fibration between topological categories that is a fiberwise fiber bundle, the same is true for the left vertical morphism.
In particular,
\widetilde{\Un}^{\pitchfork}(M)
is a topological category.
Observation
4.1.3
implies the fiber of the morphism
\widetilde{\Un}^{\pitchfork}(M)\xrightarrow{\pi}\Un^{\pitchfork}(M)
over
[p]\in\mathbf{\Delta}
is the fiber bundle
\widetilde{\cMap}^{\pitchfork}(\Delta^{p}\times M,\Th(\xi))\to\cMap^{\pitchfork}(\Delta^{p}\times M,\Th(\xi))
of Lemma
4.1.4
.
Consequently, the evaluation map (
36
) (from the proof of Lemma
4.1.6
) defines a map to
B
from the topological space of objects of
\widetilde{\Un}^{\pitchfork}(M)
:

(56)

\ev\colon\Obj(\widetilde{\Un}^{\pitchfork}(M))\xrightarrow{~(\ref{e42})~}B~.

For each morphism
[p]\xrightarrow{\sigma}[q]
in
\mathbf{\Delta}
, the diagram among topological spaces

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widetilde{\cMap}^{\pitchfork}(\Delta^{p}\times M,\Th(\xi))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widetilde{\cMap}^{\pitchfork}(\Delta^{p}\times M,\Th(\xi))\underset{\cMap^{\pitchfork}_{p}(M)}{\times}\cMap^{\pitchfork}_{q}(M)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widetilde{\cMap}^{\pitchfork}(\Delta^{q}\times M,\Th(\xi))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma_{\ast}\times M}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev}

commutes.
Indeed, an element in the upper middle topological space is of the form

\Bigl(~\Delta^{q}\times M\xrightarrow{f}\Th(\xi)~,~x\in(\sigma_{\ast}\times M)^{-1}\bigl(f^{-1}(B)\bigr)~\Bigr)~;

the value of both of the composite maps in this diagram on this element is
f\bigl((\sigma_{\ast}\times M)(x)\bigr)\in B
.
Consequently, (
56
) extends as a morphism between topological categories

(57)

\ev\colon\widetilde{\Un}^{\pitchfork}(M)\longrightarrow B~,

in which the codomain is the topological category whose topological space of objects is
B
and all of whose morphisms are identity morphisms.

As observed above, the morphism
\widetilde{\Un}^{\pitchfork}(M)\to\Un^{\pitchfork}(M)
between topological categories is a left fibration.
It is therefore classified by a functor between
\infty
-categories:

\Un^{\pitchfork}(M)\longrightarrow\Spaces~,

which, by construction, factors through
\Un^{k}(M)
.
The morphism (
57
) defines a lift of this functor:

\hbox to158.01pt{\vbox to54.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 79.0035pt\lower-27.04504pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}~.

We next argue that the diagram among
\infty
-categories

(58)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Un^{\pitchfork}(M)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Spaces_{/B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Un^{k}(M)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Spaces_{/\BO(k)}}}

commutes.

We now make use of §
6
.
Regard the rank-
k
vector bundle
\xi=(E\to B)
as a constant category-object in
{\sf VBun}^{k}
(i.e., all morphisms in it are identity morphisms).
Base-change along the morphism (
56
) defines a rank-
k
vector bundle
\ev^{\ast}\xi
over the topological category
\widetilde{\Un}^{\pitchfork}(M)
.
Recall the rank-
k
vector bundle
\nu^{\sf fib}
over the topological category
\widetilde{\Un}^{k}(M)
.
Base-change of
\nu^{\sf fib}
along the defining morphism
\widetilde{\Un}^{\pitchfork}(M)\xrightarrow{(\ref{e53})}\widetilde{\Un}^{k}(M)
defines a rank-
k
vector bundle
\nu^{\pi}
over the topological category
\widetilde{\Un}^{\pitchfork}(M)
.

The canonical identifications (
38
), applied to
C=\Delta^{p}\times M
for each
p\geq 0
, is an identification between vector bundles
\nu^{\pi}\cong\ev^{\ast}\xi
over the topological space
\Obj(\widetilde{\Un}^{\pitchfork}(M))
of objects of
\widetilde{\Un}^{\pitchfork}(M)
.
Furthermore, by way of Fact
3.6.4
, these canonical identifications (
38
) are such that the diagram among vector bundles over
\Mor(\widetilde{\Un}^{\pitchfork}(M))

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\sf s}^{\ast}\nu^{\pi}_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\sf t}^{\ast}\nu^{\pi}_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\sf s}^{\ast}\ev^{\ast}\xi}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\sf t}^{\ast}\ev^{\ast}\xi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu^{\pi}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\ref{e30})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\ref{e30})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

commutes.
We conclude an isomorphism

\nu^{\pi}~\cong~\ev^{\ast}\xi

between vector bundles over
\widetilde{\Un}^{\pitchfork}(M)
.
Using that
\widetilde{\Un}^{\pitchfork}(M)
is a topological category,
this isomorphism represents an identification

\nu^{\pi}\simeq\ev^{\ast}\xi~\colon~\widetilde{\Un}^{\pitchfork}(M)\longrightarrow\BO(k)

between
\infty
-categories.
We conclude that the diagram among
\infty
-categories (
58
) indeed commutes.

By definition of
\Un^{\xi}(M)
, the commutative diagram (
58
) is a functor between
\infty
-categories

\Un^{\pitchfork}(M)\longrightarrow\Un^{\xi}(M)~,

both of which are right fibrations over
\mathbf{\Delta}
.
The straightening of this functor is the sought morphism (
54
) between simplicial spaces:

\cMap^{\pitchfork}_{\bullet}(M,\Th(\xi))\xrightarrow{(\ref{e47})}\Bord^{\xi}_{\bullet}(M)
.

Proof of Theorem
4.0.1
.

By construction, for each
p\geq 0
, the value of the morphism (
54
) on spaces of
p
-simplices is the map between spaces

\cMap^{\pitchfork}(\Delta^{p}\times M,\Th(\xi))\xrightarrow{~(-)^{-1}(B)~}\Sub^{\xi}(\Delta^{p}\times M)

of Lemma
4.1.6
.
Theorem
4.3.2
states that this map between spaces of
p
-simplices is an equivalence.
Therefore, the morphism (
54
) between simplicial spaces is an equivalence, as desired.

Functoriality with respect to open embeddings
M\hookrightarrow M^{\prime}
follows from commutativity of the diagram of inverse images

\textstyle{\cMap^{\pitchfork}(\Delta^{p}\times M,\Th(\xi))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(-)^{-1}(B)}
\textstyle{\Sub^{\xi}(\Delta^{p}\times M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cMap^{\pitchfork}(\Delta^{p}\times M^{\prime},\Th(\xi))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(-)^{-1}(B)}
\textstyle{\Sub^{\xi}(\Delta^{p}\times M^{\prime})}

in which the right vertical map is the evident inclusion, and the left vertical map is extension by zero, i.e., it extends a compactly-supported map
\Delta^{p}\times M\rightarrow\Th(\xi)
to
\Delta^{p}\times M^{\prime}
so as to be constant at the basepoint outside of
\Delta^{p}\times M
.
∎

5.
The simplicial set
\dBord_{\bullet}^{\xi}(M)

Throughout this section, we fix
n,k\geq 0
; an
(n+k)
-manifold
M
with boundary; and a rank-
k
vector bundle
\xi=(E\to B)
.
The main object in this section is a simplicial set
\dBord^{\xi}_{\bullet}(M)
.
The main results are Corollary
5.3.7
, which states that its geometric realization agrees with that of the simplicial space
\Bord^{\xi}_{\bullet}(M)
; and Corollary
5.3.6
, which states that the simplicial set
\dBord^{\xi}_{\bullet}(M)
is a Kan complex.

5.1.
Constructing
\dBord^{\xi}_{\bullet}(M)

Throughout this subsection, we fix a manifold with corners
C
.

Notation 5.1.1
.

The set

\dSub^{k}(C)~:=~\left\{~W\subset C~\right\}

consists of compact codimension-
k
submanifolds of
C
.

Definition 5.1.2
.

The set

\dSub^{\xi}(C)~:=~\left\{\left(~W\subset C~,~W\xrightarrow{g}B~,~\nu_{W\subset C}\overset{\alpha}{\cong}g^{\ast}\xi~\right)\right\}

consists of triples in which:
(W\subset C)\in\Sub^{k}(C)
is a compact codimension-
k
submanifold;
g\in\Map(W,B)
is a map; and
\alpha\in{\sf Iso}(\nu_{W\subset C},g^{\ast}\xi)
is an isomorphism between vector bundles over
W
.

Let
D\xrightarrow{\sigma}C
be a proper face-submersion from a manifold with corners in the sense of Definition
3.2.1
.
Consider the map between sets

(59)

\dSub^{\xi}(C)\xrightarrow{~\sigma^{-1}(-)~}\dSub^{\xi}(D)~,

whose value on an element

\left(~W\subset C~,~W\xrightarrow{g}B~,~\nu_{W\subset C}\overset{\alpha}{\cong}g^{\ast}\xi~\right)

in the domain is the element

\left(~\sigma^{-1}(W)\subset D~,~\sigma^{-1}(W)\xrightarrow{g\circ\sigma_{|\sigma^{-1}(W)}}B~,~\nu_{\sigma^{-1}(W)\subset D}\overset{\sigma^{-1}(\alpha)}{\cong}(g\circ\sigma_{|\sigma^{-1}(W)})^{\ast}\xi\right)

in the codomain, in which the third coordinate
\sigma^{-1}(\alpha)
is the canonical composite isomorphism between vector bundles over
\sigma^{-1}(W)
:

\displaystyle\sigma^{-1}(\alpha)\colon\nu_{\sigma^{-1}(W)\subset D}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathsf{D}\sigma_{|\sigma^{-1}(W)}}{\cong}

\displaystyle(\sigma_{|\sigma^{-1}(W)})^{\ast}\nu_{W\subset C}

\displaystyle\overset{(\sigma_{|\sigma^{-1}(W)})^{\ast}\alpha}{\cong}

\displaystyle(\sigma_{|\sigma^{-1}(W)})^{\ast}g^{\ast}\xi

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle(g\circ\sigma_{|\sigma^{-1}(W)})^{\ast}\xi~,

in which the first isomorphism is Fact
3.6.4
(1).
Lemma
3.2.8
ensures the map (
59
) is indeed well-defined.

Observation 5.1.3
.

Let
E\xrightarrow{\tau}D\xrightarrow{\sigma}C
be a composable pair of proper face-submersions.
The diagram among sets

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\dSub^{\xi}(C)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\dSub^{\xi}(E)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\dSub^{\xi}(D)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\sigma\circ\tau)^{-1}(-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma^{-1}(-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{-1}(-)}

commutes.
Indeed, this is obviously so on the first two coordinates, and Fact
3.6.4
(2) implies it is so on the third coordinates.

Observation
5.1.3
validates the following.

Definition 5.1.4
.

We denote the simplicial sets

\dBord^{k}_{\bullet}(M)~:=~\dSub^{k}(\Delta^{\bullet}\times M)\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\dBord^{\xi}_{\bullet}(M)~:=~\dSub^{\xi}(\Delta^{\bullet}\times M)~.

Observation 5.1.5
.

The set
\dSub^{k}(C)
is that underlying the topological space
\Sub^{k}(C)
.
Therefore, the simplicial set
\dBord^{k}_{\bullet}(M)
is that underlying the simplicial topological space
\Bord^{k}_{\bullet}(M)
.

Observe the existence of a natural forgetful map

(60)

\dSub^{\xi}(C)\longrightarrow\dSub^{k}(C)~,\qquad(W,f,\alpha)\longmapsto W~.

Observation 5.1.6
.

Let
D\xrightarrow{\sigma}C
be a proper face-submersion.
The diagram among sets

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\dSub^{\xi}(C)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\dSub^{\xi}(D)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\dSub^{k}(C)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\dSub^{k}(D)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma^{-1}(-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma^{-1}(-)}

commutes.

Observation
5.1.6
yields a forgetful morphism between simplicial sets

(61)

\dBord^{\xi}_{\bullet}(M)\longrightarrow\dBord^{k}_{\bullet}(M)~.

5.2.
\Bord^{\xi}_{\bullet}(M)
as a simplicial topological space

Here, we construct a topology on the simplicial set
\dBord^{\xi}_{\bullet}(M)
whose underlying simplicial space is
\Bord^{\xi}_{\bullet}(M)
of Definition
3.5.4
.

Proposition 5.2.1
.

There is a topological space
\tSub^{\xi}(C)
with the following properties.

(1)

Its underlying set is
\dSub^{\xi}(C)
.

(2)

The resulting function
\tSub^{\xi}(C)\xrightarrow{(\ref{a2})}\Sub^{k}(C)
has the following properties.

(a)

It is continuous.

(b)

It is a Serre fibration.

(c)

The underlying space of
\tSub^{\xi}(C)
is identical with the space
\Sub^{\xi}(C)
of Definition
3.4.3
.

Proof.

Let
W
be a compact
(p+n)
-manifold with corners.
Consider the diagram among topological spaces

\hbox to399.87pt{\vbox to81.63pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 199.93248pt\lower-40.81255pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

in which the maps
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
are the evident projections.
Taking derivatives defines an injection between vector bundles over
\Emb(W,C)\times W
, which defines a short exact sequence of vector bundles:

0\longrightarrow\pr^{\ast}\tau_{W}\xrightarrow{~\mathsf{D}~}\ev^{\ast}\tau_{C}\xrightarrow{~\rm quotient~}\nu\longrightarrow 0~.

Consider the two vector bundles
\pi_{1}^{\ast}\nu
and
(\ev\circ\pi_{2})^{\ast}\xi
over
\Emb(W,C)\times\Map(W,B)\times W
.
Consider the resulting principal
{\sf GL}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
-bundle of isomorphisms between these vector bundles:

(62)

X~:=~{\sf Iso}~:=~{\sf Iso}\left(~\pi_{1}^{\ast}\nu~,~(\ev\circ\pi_{2})^{\ast}\xi~\right)\longrightarrow\Emb(W,C)\times\Map(W,B)\times W~=:~Y~.

Note that each of the maps

X\longrightarrow Y\longrightarrow\Emb(W,C)\times\Map(W,B)~=:~Z

is a fiber bundle.
Consider the fiber bundles

\Map^{\sf rel}_{Z}\left(Y,Y\right)\longrightarrow Z\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\Map^{\sf rel}_{Z}\left(Y,X\right)\longrightarrow Z~,

of relative sections of

Y\to Z

along
Y\to Z

and
of relative sections of

X\to Z

along
Y\to Z
, respectively.
4
4

4

For
(W\xrightarrow{e}C,W\xrightarrow{g}B)\in Z
, the fiber of the first fiber bundle over this point is the topological space of maps
\Map(W,W)
, while the fiber of the second fiber bundle over this point is the topological space of maps
\Map\left(W,{\sf Iso}(\nu_{e},g^{\ast}\xi)\right)
to the
{\sf GL}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
-torsor of isomorphisms between vector bundles over
W
.

Notice the canonical continuous forgetful map

(63)

\Map^{\sf rel}_{Z}\left(Y,X\right)\longrightarrow\Map^{\sf rel}_{Z}\left(Y,Y\right)

between fiber bundles over
Z
.
Because
X\to Y
is a fiber bundle, so is the map (
63
).
The identity map on
Y
is a continuous section of the codomain of (
63
).

Because fiber bundles are stable under base-change, the maps

(64)

\Emb^{\xi}(W,C)\longrightarrow Z=\Emb(W,C)\times\Map(W,B)\xrightarrow{~\rm projection~}\Emb(W,C)

are each fiber bundles.
The first of these fiber bundles is a principal
\Map(W,{\sf GL}_{n}(\mathbb{R}))
-bundle whose fiber over
(W\xrightarrow{e}C,W\xrightarrow{g}B)
is the
\Map(W,{\sf GL}_{n}(\mathbb{R}))
-torsor
{\sf Iso}(\nu_{e},g^{\ast}\xi)
of isomorphisms of vector bundles over
W
.

Necessarily, the action
\Diff(W)\lacts\Emb^{\xi}(W,C)
is free, and by slices, because the action
\Diff(W)\lacts\Emb(W,C)
is.
Therefore the vertical maps in this diagram are principal
\Diff(W)
-bundles, and this diagram is a pullback.
Using that (
64
) is a fiber bundle as well, Lemma
3.2.11
implies the lower horizontal map is a Serre fibration.
Observe the canonical bijection between sets,

\underset{[W]}{\coprod}\Emb^{\xi}(W,C)_{/\Diff(W)}\xrightarrow{~\cong~}\dSub^{\xi}(C)~,

in which the coproduct is indexed by isomorphism classes of
(n+p)
-manifolds with corners.
This bijection endows the set
\dSub^{\xi}(C)
with a topology, thereby proving statement (1) of the proposition.
Denote this topological space
\tSub^{\xi}(C)
.
Because the lower horizontal map in (
66
) is a Serre fibration, the canonical map

\tSub^{\xi}(C)\xrightarrow{~(\ref{a2})~}\Sub^{k}(C)

is a Serre fibration, thereby proving statements (2)(a) and (2)(b).
By Definition
3.4.3
of the map of space
\Sub^{\xi}(C)\to\Sub^{k}(C)
as a base-change, the pullback diagram (
65
) for each
W
then implies
Statement (2)(c) by taking
\Diff(W)
-quotients for each
W
.
∎

Convention 5.2.2
.

In what follows, we regard
\tSub^{\xi}(C)
as a topological space with the topology of Proposition
5.2.1
.

Proposition
5.2.1
(2)(b) has the following strengthening.

Lemma 5.2.3
.

The map
\tSub^{\xi}(C)\xrightarrow{(\ref{a2})}\Sub^{k}(C)
is a fiber bundle.

(W\subset C)\in\Sub^{k}(C)

Consider the map

\Sub^{\xi}(C)_{|\Gamma^{\sf sm}(\nu_{W\subset C})}\xrightarrow{~F~}\Gamma^{\sf sm}(\nu_{W\subset C})\times\tSub^{\xi}(C)_{|W}~,

whose value on an element

\bigl(~W\xrightarrow{s}E(\nu_{W\subset C})~,~\psi(s(W))\xrightarrow{g}B~,~\nu_{\psi(s(W))\subset C}\overset{\alpha}{\cong}g^{\ast}\xi~\bigr)

in the domain is the element

\bigl(~W\xrightarrow{s}E(\nu_{W\subset C})~,~W\xrightarrow{g\circ\psi\circ s}B~,~\nu_{W\subset C}\overset{\alpha\circ\mathsf{D}(\psi\circ{\sf Add}_{s}\circ\psi^{-1})}{\cong}g^{\ast}\xi~\bigr)

in the codomain, where the last coordinate is the composite isomorphism between vector bundles over
W
:

\displaystyle\nu_{W\subset C}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathsf{D}\psi^{-1}}{\cong}

\displaystyle\nu_{W\subset E(\nu_{W\subset C})}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathsf{D}{\sf Add}_{s}}{\cong}

\displaystyle\nu_{s(W)\subset E(\nu_{W\subset C})}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathsf{D}\psi}{\cong}

\displaystyle\nu_{\psi(s(W))\subset C}

\displaystyle\overset{\alpha}{\cong}

\displaystyle g^{\ast}\xi~.

Here, the second isomorphism is that induced by the morphism between affine bundles
\nu_{W\subset C}\xrightarrow{{\sf Add}_{s}}\nu_{W\subset C}
over
W
given by
(w,v)\mapsto(w,v+s(w))
, which carries the (image of the) zero-section
W\subset E(\nu_{W\subset C})
to the image of the section
s
.
The map
F
is evidently a bijection, with inverse given by

(s,g,\alpha)\longmapsto\left(~s~,~\psi(s(W))\xrightarrow{g\circ s^{-1}\circ\psi^{-1}}B~,~\alpha\circ\mathsf{D}\psi\circ\mathsf{D}{\sf Add}_{s}^{-1}\circ\mathsf{D}\psi^{-1}~\right)~.

Unpacking the topology of
\tSub^{\xi}(C)
from the proof of Proposition
5.2.1
, and thereafter of its subspaces
\tSub^{\xi}(C)_{|\Gamma^{\sf sm}(\nu_{W\subset C})}
and
\Sub^{\xi}(C)_{|W}
, reveals that both
F
and
F^{-1}
are continuous.
Therefore,
\Sub^{\xi}(C)\to\Sub^{k}(C)
is locally trivializable.

∎

Lemma
5.2.3
draws attention to the fibers of the forgetful map
\tSub^{\xi}(C)\xrightarrow{(\ref{a2})}\Sub^{k}(C)
, which we examine now.
Consider the fiber of the map (
60
) over
(W\subset C)\in\Sub^{k}(C)
,

\tSub^{\xi}(C)_{|W}~:=~\left\{\left(~W\xrightarrow{g}B~,~\nu_{W\subset C}\overset{\alpha}{\cong}g^{\ast}\xi~\right)\right\}~,

whose underlying set consists of pairs in which
g\in\Map(W,B)
is a map and
\alpha\in{\sf Iso}(\nu_{W\subset C},g^{\ast}\xi)
is an isomorphism between vector bundles over
W
.
Consider the function between underlying sets:

(67)

\tSub^{\xi}(C)_{|W}\longrightarrow\Map(W,B)~,\qquad(g,\alpha)\longmapsto g~.

Inspecting the topology on
\tSub^{\xi}(C)
reveals the following.

Observation 5.2.4
.

Let
(W\subset C)\in\Sub^{k}(C)
.
The function (
67
) is continuous.
Furthermore, the evident action
\Aut(\nu_{W\subset C})\lacts\tSub^{\xi}(C)_{|W}
is continuous and free, and the map (
67
) witnesses the quotient by
\Aut(\nu_{W\subset C})
.
Lastly, this action is by slices, and therefore the quotient map (
67
) is a principal
\Aut(\nu_{W\subset C})
-bundle.

For
D\xrightarrow{\sigma}C
a proper face-submersion,
the commutative diagram of Observation
5.1.6
supplies, for each
(W\subset C)\in\Sub^{k}(C)
, a function between underlying sets

(68)

\tSub^{\xi}(C)_{|W}\xrightarrow{~\sigma^{-1}(-)_{|}~}\tSub^{\xi}(D)_{|\sigma^{-1}(W)}~.

Lemma 5.2.5
.

Let
D\xrightarrow{\sigma}C
be a proper face-submersion.
The function (
68
) is continuous.
Furthermore, if
\sigma
is a proper face-embedding, then (
68
) is a Serre fibration.

Proof.

Now, notice that the resulting map between fibers fits into a commutative diagram among sets:

(69)

\hbox to196.19pt{\vbox to60.13pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 98.09326pt\lower-28.22395pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}

Denote the composite horizontal map
W\times\Sub^{\xi}(C)_{|W}\xrightarrow{g}B
.
By definition of
\Sub^{\xi}(C)_{|W}
, there is a canonical isomorphism
\pr^{\ast}\nu_{W\subset C}\overset{\alpha}{\cong}g^{\ast}\xi
between vector bundles over
W\times\Sub^{\xi}(C)_{|W}
.
This isomorphism pulls back as an isomorphism between vector bundles over
\sigma^{-1}(W)\times\Sub^{\xi}(C)_{|W}
,

(71)

\pr^{\ast}\nu_{\sigma^{-1}(W)\subset D}\underset{\rm Fact~\ref{trans.fact}(1)}{\cong}\pr^{\ast}{\sigma_{|}}^{\ast}\nu_{W\subset C}={\sigma_{|}}^{\ast}\pr^{\ast}\nu_{W\subset C}\overset{{\sigma_{|}}^{\ast}\alpha}{\cong}{\sigma_{|}}^{\ast}g^{\ast}\xi=G^{\ast}\xi~,

in which the equalities follow from commutativity of the diagram (
70
).
Unpacking the definition of
\Sub^{\xi}(D)_{|\sigma^{-1}(W)}
reveals how the upper horizontal map in (
69
), as it lifts the map
\Sub^{\xi}(C)_{|W}\to\Map(\sigma^{-1}(W),B)
, is adjoint to the isomorphism (
71
).
The fact that the isomorphism (
71
) is, in particular, continuous implies the upper horizontal map in (
69
) is continuous, as desired.

Generally, for
\chi=\left(E(\chi)\to B(\chi)\right)
a vector bundle, consider the fiber subbundle
\Aut^{\sf rel}(\chi)\to B(\chi)
of
\Map^{\sf rel}_{B(\chi)}(E(\chi),E(\chi))\to B(\chi)
consisting of the fiberwise linear isomorphisms.
This fiber bundle over
B(\chi)
is a group-object among such.
The topological group
\Aut(\chi)
of automorphisms of
\chi
is the topological space of sections of
\Aut^{\sf rel}(\chi)\to B(\chi)
.
Observe the canonical isomorphisms between fiber bundles over
\sigma^{-1}(W)
,

(\sigma_{|\sigma^{-1}(W)})^{\ast}\Aut^{\sf rel}(\nu_{W\subset C})~\cong~\Aut^{\sf rel}\left((\sigma_{|\sigma^{-1}(W)})^{\ast}\nu_{W\subset C}\right)~\cong~\Aut^{\sf rel}(\nu_{\sigma^{-1}(W)\subset D})~,

the first of which is direct from universal properties, and the second of which follows from Fact
3.6.4
.
Taking sections determines a continuous homomorphism:

(72)

\Aut(\nu_{W\subset C})=\Gamma\left(\Aut^{\sf rel}(\nu_{W\subset C})\right)\xrightarrow{\sigma_{|\sigma^{-1}(W)}}\Gamma\left(\sigma_{|\sigma^{-1}(W)})^{\ast}\Aut^{\sf rel}(\nu_{W\subset C}\right)\cong\Gamma\left(\Aut^{\sf rel}(\nu_{\sigma^{-1}(W)\subset D})\right)=\Aut(\nu_{\sigma^{-1}(W)\subset D})~.

Notice that the map
\tSub^{\xi}(C)_{|W}\xrightarrow{\sigma^{-1}(-)}\tSub^{\xi}(D)_{|\sigma^{-1}(W)}
between fibers is equivariant with respect to the continuous homomorphism
\Aut(\nu_{W\subset C})\xrightarrow{(\ref{x35})}\Aut(\nu_{\sigma^{-1}(W)\subset D})
.

Now assume
\sigma
is a proper face-embedding.
The diagram (
69
) determines a commutative diagram among topological spaces,

in which
\square
is the pullback of the right square, and the map to it is the canonical one as such.
The existence of collar neighborhoods for manifolds with corners ensures the map
D\xrightarrow{\sigma}C
is a cofibration.
Consequently, the map
\Map(W,B)\xrightarrow{-\circ\sigma_{|\sigma^{-1}(W)}}\Map(\sigma^{-1}(W),B)
is a Serre fibration.
Because Serre fibrations are stable under base-change, it follows that the map
\square\to\tSub^{\xi}(D)_{|\sigma^{-1}(W)}
is a Serre fibration.
Similarly, the map (
72
) is a Serre fibration.
Together with Observation
5.2.4
, it follows that the map
\tSub^{\xi}(C)_{|W}\to\square
is locally a Serre fibration.
Using paracompactness and Hausdorffness of mapping spaces and of
\Sub^{k}(D)
(Lemma
3.1.11
) and thereafter of
\Sub^{k}(D)_{|\sigma^{-1}(W)}
,
Theorem 13 of Section 7 of Chapter 2 of
[
Sp
]
then implies the map
\tSub^{\xi}(C)_{|W}\to\square
is a Serre fibration.
Using that the composition of Serre fibrations is a Serre fibration, we conclude that the map
\tSub^{\xi}(C)_{|W}\to\tSub^{\xi}(D)_{|\sigma^{-1}(W)}
is a Serre fibration, as desired.

∎

Lemma 5.2.6
.

Let
D\xrightarrow{\sigma}C
be a proper face-submersion.
The map
\tSub^{\xi}(C)\xrightarrow{\sigma^{-1}(-)}\tSub^{\xi}(D)
is continuous.
Furthermore, if
\sigma
is a proper face-embedding, then this map is a Serre fibration.

Proof.

59
), is continuous at each element
(W,f,\alpha)\in\tSub^{\xi}(C)

{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-24.31204pt}{-11.49236pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{64}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}~.

Choose compatible tubular neighborhoods of
W\subset C
and of
\sigma^{-1}(W)\subset D
as in the proof of Lemma
3.2.9
.
The square is then the base-change (in the category
\Ar(\Sets)
) of the square (
17
) along
\left(\tSub^{\xi}(C)\xrightarrow{\sigma^{-1}(-)}\tSub^{\xi}(D)\right)\to\left(\Sub^{k}(C)\xrightarrow{\sigma^{-1}(-)}\Sub^{k}(D)\right)
.
The two bijections, which are homeomorphisms, are those from the proof of Lemma
5.2.3
.
Now, inspecting the definition of each map in the top horizontal composite

\Gamma^{\sf sm}(\nu_{W\subset C})\times\tSub^{\xi}(C)_{|W}~\cong~\tSub^{\xi}(C)_{|\Gamma^{\sf sm}(\nu_{W\subset C})}\xrightarrow{(\ref{x31})}\tSub^{\xi}(D)_{|\Gamma^{\sf sm}(\nu_{\sigma^{-1}(W)\subset D})}~\cong~\Gamma^{\sf sm}(\nu_{\sigma^{-1}(W)\subset D})\times\tSub^{\xi}(D)_{|\sigma^{-1}(W)}

reveals that it
is a product of the map (
16
) between spaces of smooth sections and the map (
69
) between fibers.
Lemma
3.1.10
(1) implies the vertical maps are open embeddings.
Continuity of (
59
) at
(W,f,\alpha)\in\tSub^{\xi}(C)
then follows from the continuity of the map (
16
) and Lemma
5.2.5
.

Now suppose
D\xrightarrow{\sigma}C
is a proper face-embedding.
We may factor the map
\tSub^{\xi}(C)\xrightarrow{\sigma^{-1}(-)}\tSub^{\xi}(D)
as the top horizontal map in the commutative diagram among topological spaces:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tSub^{\xi}(C)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\square}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tSub^{\xi}(D)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Sub^{k}(C)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Sub^{k}(C)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Sub^{k}(D)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma^{-1}(-)}

where
\square
makes the right square pullback the map to it is the canonical one as such.
Because Serre fibrations are stable under base-change, Lemma
3.2.10
implies the map
\square\to\tSub^{\xi}(D)
is a Serre fibration.
Because Serre fibrations are stable under composition, the map
\tSub^{\xi}(C)\xrightarrow{\sigma^{-1}(-)}\tSub^{\xi}(D)
is a Serre fibration provided the map
\tSub^{\xi}(C)\to\square
is a Serre fibration.
Because fiber bundles are stable under base-change, Lemma
5.2.3
implies the middle vertical map is a fiber bundle.
As argued above, the base-change of
\tSub^{\xi}(C)\to\square
along the open embedding
\Gamma^{\sf sm}(\nu_{W\subset C})\hookrightarrow\Sub^{k}(C)
is homeomorphic with a product of maps

\Gamma^{\sf sm}(\nu_{W\subset C})\times\tSub^{\xi}(C)_{|W}\xrightarrow{~\id\times(\ref{x34})~}\Gamma^{\sf sm}(\nu_{W\subset C})\times\tSub^{\xi}(D)_{|\sigma^{-1}(W)}~.

Lemma
5.2.5
states that (
69
) is a Serre fibration.
Therefore, the map
\tSub^{\xi}(C)\to\square
is, locally over
\square
, a Serre fibration.
Lemma
3.1.11
implies the topological space
\square
is metrizable, and therefore paracompact and Hausdorff. A local Serre fibration is Serre fibration if the base is paracompact and Hausdorff (e.g., see Theorem 13, §2.7 of
[
Sp
]
), so the map
\tSub^{\xi}(C)\to\square
is therefore a Serre fibration, as desired.

∎

Lemma
5.2.6
justifies the following.

Definition 5.2.7
.

The simplicial topological space
\tBord^{\xi}_{\bullet}(M)
is

\tBord^{\xi}_{\bullet}(M)~:=~\tSub^{\xi}(\Delta^{\bullet}\times M)~.

Lemma
5.2.1
(2)(c) immediately implies the following.

Corollary 5.2.8
.

The underlying simplicial space of the simplicial topological space of Definition
5.2.7
is identical with that of Definition
3.5.4

5.3.
The map
\dBord_{\bullet}^{\xi}(M)\rightarrow\Bord_{\bullet}^{\xi}(M)
is a trivial Kan fibration

Here, we prove that the simplicial set
\dBord^{\xi}_{\bullet}(M)
is a Kan complex, and that its geometric realization is identical with that of
\Bord^{\xi}_{\bullet}(M)
.

Let
C
be a manifold with corners satisfying the property that the closure of each face is a manifold with corners in its own right.
Let
C_{0}\subset C
be a closed union of faces of a manifold with corners.
Consider the poset
\mathcal{P}(C_{0})
in which an element is a face of
C
that is contained in
C_{0}
, and whose partial order is given by
E\leq F
if there is containment in the closure:
E\subset\overline{F}
.
Note that, if
C_{0}\subset C
is the closure of a face, then
\mathcal{P}(C_{0})
has a final object.
The assumption on
C
ensures, for
E\leq F
in
\mathcal{P}(C_{0})
, that the inclusion between closures
\overline{E}\hookrightarrow\overline{F}
is a proper face-embedding between manifolds with corners.
Consider the functor

\mathcal{P}(C_{0})^{\op}\xrightarrow{~\Sub^{k}(-)~}\Top^{\Sub^{k}(C)/}

whose value on
F\in\mathcal{P}(C_{0})
is the map
\Sub^{k}(C)\xrightarrow{(\ref{e56})}\Sub^{k}(\overline{F})
which Lemma
3.2.9
ensures is continuous.
Functoriality of this assignment follows from the standard identity in set theory,
(\tau\circ\sigma)^{-1}(W)=\sigma^{-1}(\tau^{-1}(W))
.
This functor determines a canonical map to the limit topological space

\Sub^{k}(C)\longrightarrow\underset{F\in\mathcal{P}(C_{0})^{\op}}{\limit}\Sub^{k}(\overline{F})~=:~\Sub^{k}(C_{0})~.

Denote the underlying set of this limit as
\dSub^{k}(C_{0})
.
Consider the commutative diagram among topological spaces,

\hbox to143.72pt{\vbox to54.62pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 71.86044pt\lower-27.3095pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

in which the downward maps are given by intersection with
C_{0}
and the rightward maps are the identity maps on underlying sets.
This commutative diagram determines a map to the homotopy pullback:

(73)

\dSub^{k}(C)\longrightarrow\dSub^{k}(C_{0})\underset{\Sub^{k}(C_{0})}{\times^{\sf h}}\Sub^{k}(C)~.

Lemma 5.3.1
.

Let
C
be a manifold with corners satisfying the property that the closure of each face is a manifold with corners in its own right.
Let
C_{0}\subset C
be a closed union of faces.
The map (
73
) is surjective on path-components.

Proof.

Recall that the homotopy pullback of the diagram
\dSub^{k}(C_{0})\rightarrow\Sub^{k}(C_{0})\leftarrow\Sub^{k}(C)
among topological spaces may be defined as the limit of the diagram among topological spaces

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dSub^{k}(C_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Sub^{k}(C_{0})^{I}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sub^{k}(C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sub^{k}(C_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sub^{k}(C_{0})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev_{1}}

where
\Sub^{k}(C_{0})^{I}:=\Map(I,\Sub^{k}(C_{0}))
is the set of maps from the closed interval
I:=[0,1]
to
\Sub^{k}(C_{0})
, endowed with the compact-open topology, and where
\ev_{i}
is evaluation at
i\in\{0,1\}
.
Unpacking this description of the homotopy pullback in the case at hand lends to the following description of a point in the codomain of (
73
):

+

a compact codimension-
k
submanifold

\widetilde{W}\subset C~,

+

a fiber bundle over
I
of compact codimension-
k
submanifolds

W_{I}\subset I\times C_{0}~;

such that the two subsets of
C_{0}=\{0\}\times C_{0}
agree:

-

\widetilde{W}\cap C_{0}=W_{I}\cap(\{0\}\times C_{0})
.

By smooth approximation, we may homotope the continuous map
I\to\Sub^{k}(C_{0})
classifying
W_{I}\subset I\times C_{0}
to assume
W_{I}\subset I\times C_{0}
is a submanifold.

Through this description of a point in the codomain of the map (
73
), this map is given by

(\widetilde{W}\subset C)\longmapsto\Bigl(~(\widetilde{W}\subset C)~,~(I\times(\widetilde{W}\cap C_{0}))~\Bigr)~.

Evidently, this map (
73
) is injective, and its image consists of those
(\widetilde{W},W_{I})
for which
W_{I}=I\times(\widetilde{W}\cap C_{0})
.

Now, let
(\widetilde{W}\subset C,W_{I}\subset I\times C_{0})
be a point in the codomain of (
73
).
In what follows, we will construct a compact subspace
\widetilde{W}_{I}\subset I\times C
that satisfies the following properties:

(1)

The subspace

\widetilde{W}_{I}~\subset~I\times C

is a fiber bundle over
I
of compact codimension-
k
submanifolds of
I\times C
.

(2)

The two subsets of
I\times C_{0}
agree:

W_{I}~=~\widetilde{W}_{I}\cap(I\times C_{0})~.

(3)

The two subsets of
C=\{0\}\times C
agree:

\widetilde{W}~=~\widetilde{W}_{I}\cap(\{0\}\times C)~.

Given such a
\widetilde{W}_{I}\subset I\times C
, then
(\widetilde{W}_{I}\cap(\{1\}\times C)\subset\{1\}\times C=C)\in\dSub^{k}(C)
, and
\widetilde{W}_{I}
witnesses a path in the codomain of (
73
) from its image to the given element
(\widetilde{W}\subset C,W_{I}\subset I\times C_{0})
.
Therefore, the lemma is proved upon constructing such a
\widetilde{W}_{I}\subset I\times C
.

Consider the normal vector bundle
\nu_{\widetilde{W}}
of
\widetilde{W}\subset C
.
Through the tubular neighborhood theorem, choose an open neighborhood
\widetilde{W}\subset\widetilde{N}\subset C
together with a diffeomorphism under
\widetilde{W}
from the total space
E(\nu_{\widetilde{W}})\cong\widetilde{N}
.
Similarly, consider the normal vector bundle
\nu_{W_{I}}
of
W_{I}\subset I\times C_{0}
.
Through the tubular neighborhood theorem, choose an open neighborhood
W_{I}\subset N_{I}\subset I\times C_{0}
together with a diffeomorphism under
W_{I}
and over
I
from the total space
E(\nu_{W_{I}})\cong N_{I}
.
Denote the compact codimension-
k
submanifold
W_{0}=\widetilde{W}\cap C_{0}=W_{I}\cap(\{0\}\times C_{0})\subset C_{0}
.
Upon shrinking and reparametrizing as needed, we may assume
N_{0}:=\widetilde{N}\cap C_{0}=N_{I}\cap(\{0\}\times C_{0})\subset C_{0}
and the composite isomorphism

E(\nu_{W_{0}})~\cong~E(\nu_{\widetilde{W}})_{|W_{0}}~\cong~\widetilde{N}\cap C_{0}=N_{I}\cap(\{0\}\times C_{0})~\cong~E(\nu_{W_{I}})_{|W_{0}}~\cong~E(\nu_{W_{0}})

is the identity.
Consider the compact codimension-
k
submanifold
I\times\widetilde{W}\subset I\times C
.
These data determine a tubular neighborhood
I\times\widetilde{W}\subset I\times\widetilde{N}\subset I\times C
together with a diffeomorphism
E(\nu_{I\times\widetilde{W}})=I\times E(\nu_{\widetilde{W}})\cong I\times\widetilde{N}
, which is
under
I\times\widetilde{W}
and over
I
from the total space
E(\nu_{W_{I}})\cong N_{I}
.
These data, in turn, determine smooth retractions

\pi_{I}\colon N_{I}\cong E(\nu_{W_{I}})\to W_{I}~,\qquad\widetilde{\pi}\colon\widetilde{N}\cong E(\nu_{\widetilde{W}})\to\widetilde{W}~,\text{ and }\qquad\pi_{0}\colon N_{0}\cong E(\nu_{W_{0}})\to W_{0}~,

the last of which is a common restriction of each of the first two.

Containment Case.

We now prove the lemma in the case that there is containment between subsets of
I\times C_{0}
,

W_{I}~\subset~I\times N_{0}~,

and the resulting map over
I
,

\pr\colon W_{I}~\hookrightarrow~I\times N_{0}\xrightarrow{~I\times\pi_{0}~}I\times W_{0}~,

is a diffeomorphism.
In this case, the composite map

(74)

I\times W_{0}\xrightarrow{~\pr^{-1}~}W_{I}\hookrightarrow I\times N_{0}~\cong~I\times E(\nu_{W_{0}})~=~E(\nu_{I\times W_{0}})

is a smooth section of the normal vector bundle
\nu_{I\times W_{0}}
over
I\times W_{0}\subset I\times C_{0}
.
Consider the zero-section

(75)

\widetilde{W}\xrightarrow{~\rm zero~}E(\nu_{\widetilde{W}})~,

which is a smooth section of the normal vector bundle
\nu_{\widetilde{W}}
over
\widetilde{W}\subset C
.
Observe that (
74
) restricts along
W_{0}\hookrightarrow I\times W_{0}
as the zero-section.
So the sections (
74
) and (
75
), together, define a smooth section
of
\nu_{I\times\widetilde{W}}
over the closed union of faces
(I\times W_{0})\cup\widetilde{W}\subset I\times\widetilde{W}
:

(76)

\widetilde{W}\cup W_{I}\xrightarrow{~(\ref{x22})\cup(\ref{x23})~}E(\nu_{I\times\widetilde{W}})~.

Now, using compactness of
I\times\widetilde{W}
, choose a smooth vector bundle injection
\nu_{I\times\widetilde{W}}\hookrightarrow\epsilon^{D}_{I\times\widetilde{W}}
into a high-rank trivial vector bundle over
I\times\widetilde{W}
; choose, additionally, a smooth vector bundle retraction
\epsilon^{D}_{I\times\widetilde{W}}\to\nu_{I\times\widetilde{W}}
to this injection.
Lemma
2.3.3
then implies the section (
76
) extends as a smooth section:

(77)

I\times\widetilde{W}\longrightarrow E(\nu_{I\times\widetilde{W}})~.

We then have a composite smooth embedding:

(78)

I\times\widetilde{W}\xrightarrow{~(\ref{x25})~}E(\nu_{I\times\widetilde{W}})~\cong~I\times\widetilde{N}~\subset~I\times C~.

The image of (
78
),

\widetilde{W}_{I}~:=~{\sf Image}(\ref{x26})~\subset~I\times C~,

is a compact codimension-
k
submanifold of
I\times C
.
By construction,
\widetilde{W}_{I}\cap(I\times C_{0})=W_{I}\subset I\times C_{0}
and
\widetilde{W}_{I}\cap(\{0\}\times C)=\widetilde{W}\subset C
.
Additionally, because
\widetilde{W}_{I}
is the image of the section of the normal vector bundle of the (trivial) fiber bundle
I\times\widetilde{W}\subset I\times C
, then
\widetilde{W}_{I}\subset I\times C
is a fiber bundle over
I
of compact codimension-
k
submanifolds of
C
.
This completes the proof of the lemma in this Containment Case.

General Case.

We now prove the lemma in the general case, which will reduce to the Containment Case.
Next, using that
W_{I}\subset I\times C_{0}
is a fiber bundle over
I
of compact codimension-
k
submanifolds of
C_{0}
, choose
0=t_{0}<t_{1}<\cdots<t_{R}=1
such that, for each
0<k\leq R
, the following conditions are satisfied.

(1)

There containment between subsets of
[t_{k-1},t_{k}]\times C_{0}
:

W_{[t_{k-1},t_{k}]}~:=~W_{I}\cap\Bigl(\left[t_{k-1},t_{k}\right]\Bigr)\times C_{0}~\subset~\left[t_{k-1},t_{k}\right]\times\Bigl(N_{I}\cap\Bigl(\left\{t_{k-1}\right\}\times C_{0}\Bigr)\Bigr)~.

(2)

The resulting composite map over
[t_{k-1},t_{k}]
,

\pr_{k}\colon W_{[t_{k-1},t_{k}]}\hookrightarrow[t_{k-1},t_{k}]\times(N_{I}\cap(\{t_{k-1}\}\times C_{0}))\xrightarrow{~[t_{k-1},t_{k}]\times(\pi_{I})_{|}~}[t_{k-1},t_{k}]\times(W_{I}\cap(\{t_{k-1}\}\times C_{0}))

is a diffeomorphism.

We now inductively construct, for each
0\leq k\leq R
, a compact subspace
\widetilde{W}_{[0,t_{k}]}\subset[0,t_{k}]\times C

that satisfies the following properties:

(1)

The subspace

\widetilde{W}_{[0,t_{k}]}~\subset~[0,t_{k}]\times C

is a fiber bundle over
[0,t_{k}]
of compact codimension-
k
submanifolds of
[0,t_{k}]\times C
.

(2)

The two subsets of
[0,t_{k}]\times C_{0}
agree:

W_{I}\cap([0,t_{k}]\times C_{0})~=~\widetilde{W}_{[0,t_{k}]}\cap([0,t_{k}]\times C_{0})~.

(3)

The two subsets of
C=\{0\}\times C
agree:

\widetilde{W}~=~\widetilde{W}_{[0,t_{k}]}\cap(\{0\}\times C)~.

Note that the case
k=R
is, then, the sought data.
So completing this inductive construction completes the proof of the lemma.

Necessarily,
\widetilde{W}_{[0,t_{0}]}=\widetilde{W}
, which supplies the base case of
k=0
for the inductive construction.
Now assume
k>0
, and such a
\widetilde{W}_{[0,t_{k-1}]}\subset[0,t_{k-1}]\times C
has been constructed.
The Containment Case supplies a compact codimension-
k
submanifold
\widetilde{W}_{[t_{k-1},t_{k}]}\subset[t_{k-1},t_{k}]\times C
that satisfies the following properties:

(1)

The subspace

\widetilde{W}_{[t_{k-1},t_{k}]}~\subset~[t_{k-1},t_{k}]\times C

is a fiber bundle over
[t_{k-1},t_{k}]
of compact codimension-
k
submanifolds of
[t_{k-1},t_{k}]\times C
.

(2)

The two subsets of
[t_{k-1},t_{k}]\times C_{0}
agree:

W_{I}\cap([t_{k-1},t_{k}]\times C_{0})~=~\widetilde{W}_{[t_{k-1},t_{k}]}\cap([t_{k-1},t_{k}]\times C_{0})~.

(3)

The two subsets of
C=\{t_{k-1}\}\times C
agree:

\widetilde{W}_{[0,t_{k-1}]}\cap(\{t_{k-1}\}\times C)~=~\widetilde{W}_{[t_{k-1},t_{k}]}\cap(\{t_{k-1}\}\times C)~.

Denote the union

\widetilde{W}_{[0,t_{k}]}~:=~\widetilde{W}_{[0,t_{k-1}]}\cup\widetilde{W}_{[t_{k-1},t_{k}]}~\subset~[0,t_{k}]\times C~.

This subspace
\widetilde{W}_{[0,t_{k}]}\subset[0,t_{k}]\times C
is a compact codimension-
k
subspace that, by construction, satisfies the three named conditions.

∎

Corollary 5.3.2
.

The map of simplicial spaces

\dBord_{\bullet}^{k}(M)\longrightarrow\Bord_{\bullet}^{k}(M)

is a trivial Kan fibration.

Proof.

Surjectivity of path-components is exactly the statement of Lemma
5.3.1
applied to
(C_{0}\subset C)=(\partial\Delta^{p}\times M\subset\Delta^{p}\times M)
.

∎

We have following
\xi
-structured version of Lemma
5.3.1
.

Lemma 5.3.3
.

Let
C
be a manifold with corners satisfying the property that the closure of each face is a manifold with corners in its own right.
Let
C_{0}\subset C
be a closed union of faces.
The map

\dSub^{\xi}(C)\longrightarrow\dSub^{\xi}(C_{0})\underset{\tSub^{\xi}(C_{0})}{\times^{\sf h}}\tSub^{\xi}(C)

is surjective on path-components.

Proof.

We have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\dSub^{\xi}(C)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{s}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\dSub^{\xi}(C_{0})\underset{\Sub^{\xi}(C_{0})}{\times^{\sf h}}\Sub^{\xi}(C)}
\textstyle{\dSub^{k}(C)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s}
\textstyle{\dSub^{k}(C_{0})\underset{\Sub^{k}(C_{0})}{\times^{\sf h}}\Sub^{k}(C)}

Given the
\pi_{0}
-surjectivity the bottom horizontal arrow, to show
\pi_{0}
-surjectivity of the top arrow it suffices to check
\pi_{0}
-surjectivity of the fiber for each
(W\subset C)\in\dSub^{k}(C)
. An element

That is, it suffices to show
\pi_{0}
-surjectivity

\Bigl\{\bigl(W\xrightarrow{g}B,g^{\ast}\xi\overset{\alpha}{\cong}\nu_{W\subset C}\bigr)\Bigr\}\longrightarrow\Bigl(\dSub^{k}(C_{0})\underset{\Sub^{k}(C_{0})}{\times^{\sf h}}\Sub^{k}(C)\Bigr)_{|s(W\subset C)}

from the set of
\xi
-structures on
(W\subset C)
to the fiber of topright homotopy-pullback over the image
s(W\subset C)
. Choose an element in this fiber, which is indexed by the set of
\xi
-structures on
\{0\}\times W_{0}\subset\{0\}\subset C_{0}
, together with an extension to a
\xi
-structure on
I\times W_{0}\subset I\times C_{0}
and a
\xi
-structure on
W\subset C
, agreeing on
\{1\}\times W_{0}\subset\{1\}\times C
. Since the right vertical map is a fibration by by Lemma
5.2.1
(2)(b), this fiber is the space of sections (fixed on
\{0\}\times W_{0}
) of a fibration over the space

I\times W_{0}\underset{\{1\}\times W_{0}}{\bigcup}\{1\}\times W~.

The inclusion

I\times W_{0}\underset{\{1\}\times W_{0}}{\bigcup}\{1\}\times W\hookrightarrow I\times W

is an acyclic cofibration relative to
\{0\}\times W_{0}
, therefore there exists an extension of this
\xi
-structure to
I\times W\subset I\times C
. Restricting this to
\{0\}\times W\subset\{0\}\times C
gives the desired lift in
\dSub^{\xi}(C)
.

∎

Applying Lemma
5.3.3
to
(C_{0}\subset C)=(\partial\Delta^{p}\times M\subset\Delta^{p}\times M)
gives the following.

Corollary 5.3.4
.

The map of simplicial spaces

\dBord_{\bullet}^{\xi}(M)\longrightarrow\Bord_{\bullet}^{\xi}(M)

is a trivial Kan fibration.

Remark 5.3.5
.

Reedy fibrancy has the following surprising property:
If a simplicial topological space
X_{\bullet}
is Reedy fibrant, then the natural map from the underlying simplicial set, endowed with the discrete topology,

\delta X_{\bullet}\longrightarrow X_{\bullet}

is a trivial Kan fibration of simplicial spaces. It thereby induces an equivalence of realizations:
|\delta X_{\bullet}|\simeq|X_{\bullet}|
. We expect that the simplicial topological space
\Bord_{\bullet}^{\xi}(M)
is Reedy fibrant, which would provide a slightly different proof of Corollary
5.3.4
, but we have found it more convenient to prove the trivial Kan fibration property directly.

Corollary 5.3.6
.

The simplicial sets
\dBord^{k}_{\bullet}(M)
and
\dBord_{\bullet}^{\xi}(M)
are both Kan complexes.

Proof.

Consider the canonical morphisms between simplicial spaces:

\dBord^{k}_{\bullet}(M)\to\Bord^{k}_{\bullet}(M)\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\dBord^{\xi}_{\bullet}(M)\to\Bord^{\xi}_{\bullet}(M)~.

Corollaries
5.3.2
and
5.3.4
imply these morphisms are Kan fibrations.
Corollary
4.0.2
states that the codomains satisfy the Kan condition.
Now, observe that the composition of Kan fibrations is a Kan fibration;
observe that a simplicial space satisfies the Kan condition precisely if its unique map to the terminal simplicial space is a Kan fibration.
Consequently, the simplicial space
\dBord^{\xi}_{\bullet}(M)
satisfies the Kan condition.
As the simplicial spaces
\dBord^{k}_{\bullet}(M)
and
\dBord^{\xi}_{\bullet}(M)
are simplicial sets, we conclude that they are both Kan complexes.

∎

Corollaries
5.3.2
and
5.3.4
enable Lemma
2.5.4
to imply the following.

Corollary 5.3.7
.

The forgetful morphism between simplicial spaces

\dBord_{\bullet}^{\xi}(M)\longrightarrow\Bord_{\bullet}^{\xi}(M)

induces an equivalence between geometric realizations

\bigl|\dBord_{\bullet}^{\xi}(M)\bigr|~\simeq~\bigl|\Bord_{\bullet}^{\xi}(M)\bigr|\underset{\rm Thm~\ref{t26}}{~\simeq~}\cMap(M,\Th(\xi)).

6.
Appendix: vector bundles over topological categories

The function of this section is to give one solution to the following problem.

Let
\mathsf{C}
be a topological category.

–

Find explicit data representing a functor
\mathsf{C}\to\BO(k)
from its underlying
\infty
-category.

–

Given such data
\chi
and
\eta
, find explicit data representing an equivalence between their associated functors
\mathsf{C}\to\BO(k)
.

Here and throughout, for
\mathcal{X}
an
\infty
-category (such as
\spaces
or the ordinary category
\Top
), the
\infty
-category of
category-objects in
\mathcal{X}
is the full
\infty
-subcategory
\Cat[\mathcal{X}]\subset\Fun(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op},\mathcal{X})
consisting of those simplicial objects that satisfy the Segal conditions.
For
\mathsf{C}
a category-object in
\mathcal{X}
, we denote the two face maps

{\sf s},{\sf t}\colon\Mor(\mathsf{C}):=\mathsf{C}([1])\longrightarrow\mathsf{C}([0])=:\Obj(\mathsf{C})~,

and refer to them, respectively, as the source and the target morphisms of
\mathsf{C}
.
A
topological category
is a category-object
\mathsf{C}\in\Cat[\Top]
in
\Top
with the property that the composite functor,

\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}\xrightarrow{~\Hom([\bullet],\mathsf{C})~}\Top\xrightarrow{~\rm forget~}\Spaces~,

is a Segal space: in other words, for each
r>0
, the canonical downward map

is a weak homotopy equivalence.
Here, the superscript

Note that
\mathsf{C}
is a topological category if either the source or target map

is a Serre fibration.
If
\mathsf{C}

-category underlying this Segal space again by

.

Consider the category

{\sf VBun}^{k}

in which an object is a rank-
k
vector bundle, and a morphism is a Cartesian morphism between vector bundles (i.e., a morphism between vector bundles that is a fiberwise isomorphism).
Reporting the base of a vector bundle defines a functor

{\sf VBun}^{k}\xrightarrow{~\rm base~}\Top~,

which is a right fibration.
This functor preserves and creates finite limits.
In particular, it carries category-objects in
{\sf VBun}^{k}
to category-objects in
\Top
:

(79)

\Cat[{\sf VBun}^{k}]\xrightarrow{~\Cat[{\rm base}]~}\Cat[\Top]~.

Furthermore, (
79
) is a right fibration.
For
\mathsf{C}
a category-object in
\Top
, the groupoid

{\sf VBun}^{k}_{\mathsf{C}}

of

k
vector bundles over
\mathsf{C}
is the fiber of (
79
) over
\mathsf{C}
.

Let
\mathsf{C}
be a category-object in
\Top
.
The source and target maps of
\mathsf{C}
is a span of topological spaces:

\Obj(\mathsf{C})\xleftarrow{~{\sf s}={\sf d}_{1}~}\Mor(\mathsf{C})\xrightarrow{~{\sf t}={\sf d}_{0}~}\Obj(\mathsf{C})~.

More generally, for
[p]\xrightarrow{\delta_{i}}[p+1]
the inclusion of the complement of the
i\in[p+1]
, we denote the restriction map

{\sf d}_{i}\colon\mathsf{C}([p+1])\longrightarrow\mathsf{C}([p])~.

Note the identity
{\sf d}_{i}\circ{\sf d}_{j}={\sf d}_{j=1}\circ{\sf d}_{i}
for each
0\leq i<j\leq p
.

for
\chi=(\chi_{0},\chi_{1})
and
\eta=(\eta_{0},\eta_{1})
two such vector bundles over
\mathsf{C}
, an isomorphism
\chi\cong\eta
is

–

an isomorphism
\chi_{0}\overset{\alpha}{\cong}\eta_{0}
between vector bundles over
\Obj(\mathsf{C})
;

such that the diagram among vector bundles over
\Mor(\mathsf{C})

commutes.

Provided
\mathsf{C}
is a topological category, forgetting to underlying
\infty
-categories defines a functor from the groupoid of vector bundles over
\mathsf{C}
to the
\infty
-groupoid of functors to
\BO(k)
:

{\sf VBun}^{k}_{\mathsf{C}}\xrightarrow{~\rm forget~}\Fun(\mathsf{C},\BO(k))~.
